# Graphify module map - 2026-09-04

Source graph: `graphify-out/graph.json` (40,595 nodes), regenerated 2026-09-04.
Base commit: `e1541f21d` on branch `fix/ruida-driver`.

Built by 12 parallel subsystem agents, each required to run `graphify query`/
`explain`/`path` first and then confirm every path against the real file tree.
`inbound_refs` are verified `path` / `path:line` citations; an empty list means
the agent verified there are none.

This map is the citation source for the removal inventory (Package A0) and for
every package plan that follows. No removal or edit is planned without the
module's inbound references appearing here.

<a id="camera"></a>

## camera

### Summary

The camera subsystem is a self-contained vertical slice: a pure-data `Camera` model (persisted inside the Machine YAML), a threaded `CameraController` that owns the OpenCV capture loop and homography warping, a `CameraManager` that reconciles live controllers against `config.machine.cameras`, and a ChArUco lens-calibration package. It is reached from the app through exactly three doors: the machine settings dialog's "Camera" page (`settings_dialog.py:199-206,250`), step 10 of the unified machine wizard (`unified_wizard.py:65,229,584`), and the canvas camera-image overlay wired via `WorkSurface._sync_camera_elements` (`canvas2d/surface.py:1409`) plus the `win.toggle_camera_view` action. Nothing in the core document/pipeline/job path imports it; the only non-UI model coupling is `Machine.cameras` (list, add/remove, to_dict/from_dict) and `MachinePanel._reproject_cameras`, which re-projects `camera.image_to_world` when bed orientation or extents change. `CameraManager` is lazily constructed by `RayforgeContext.camera_mgr` (`context.py:260-268`) and shut down at `context.py:402`. There is a second, entirely unrelated "camera" in the repo — `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/camera.py`, `sim3d/camera_controller.py`, `sim3d/render_context/camera.py` — that is the 3D viewport camera and is NOT part of this subsystem.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Deleting the camera subsystem wholesale would break the app in five places, in this order of severity. (1) rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:18-19 imports Camera and migrate_camera_data at module top level — the Machine model would fail to import, taking the entire application with it. Machine.cameras (:152), add_camera/remove_camera (:1095-1103), to_dict (:1504) and from_dict (:1797-1799) all reference Camera, and every persisted machine YAML carries a `cameras:` key (confirmed in tests/config/machines/*.yaml and tests/machine/configs/machine.yaml:8). (2) rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:11-12 has the same top-level imports, plus DeviceProfile.cameras (:232, :277-279, :321, :470-473) and the LightBurn .lbdev importer path (lightburn_importer.py:190-226, 318-321) which parses cameraMatrix/distortion into a camera dict — tests/machine/device/test_lbdev_importer.py:316-355 asserts on it. (3) rayforge/machine/models/machine_panel.py:127-141 reprojects camera.image_to_world on orientation/extent change; without cameras this loop is harmless but the docstrings at :81 and :112 become lies. (4) rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:9,29 imports CameraController and CameraImageElement at module top level — the 2D work surface would fail to import. (5) The sketcher builtin addon (sketchcanvas.py:9,14; studio.py:234-249,399-400,565-569) imports both rayforge.camera.controller and rayforge.ui_gtk.canvas2d.elements.camera_image directly, so it breaks too.

SAFE REMOVAL ORDER, leaf-first: (a) scripts/screenshot/machine_settings_camera.py plus its 6 target entries in scripts/screenshot/cli.py:53-62 and "camera" in scripts/screenshot/ui_audit.py:78; (b) tests/camera/** (5 files, no inbound refs); (c) the sketcher addon's camera button and sync (studio.py:234-249, 399-400, 565-569; sketchcanvas.py camera block :83, 93-159); (d) the canvas overlay — CameraImageElement plus surface.py:1104-1145, 1409-1431 and the z-order special-cases at :1041, :1065, then the toggle chain actions.py:55,270 / visibility_overlay.py:80-95,148,170 / mainwindow.py:347-349, 872-879, 982-987, 1481-1485 and CanvasViewState.show_camera at core/config.py:37; (e) the UI package rayforge/ui_gtk/camera/** together with settings_dialog.py:9-10,18,198-206,250,261-271,389-426 and unified_wizard.py:22-24,34,39,65,228-229,304-306,340-341,483-485,562-595 plus wizard_pages/camera_page.py and review_page.py:181; (f) rayforge/camera/manager.py with context.py:9,65,260-268,402-403; (g) last, rayforge/camera/models/, calibration/, controller.py, v4l.py — and only after machine.py, profile.py, machine_panel.py and lightburn_importer.py have been de-camera'd, which is a machine-persistence change, not a camera change, and would need a config-migration story for existing users' `cameras:` YAML keys.

Two traps worth flagging. First, rayforge/camera/v4l.py is not really camera-only in effect: migrate_camera_data sits on the Machine deserialization path, so it must be removed in the same commit as machine.py:1799 and profile.py:473 or old configs silently stop migrating. Second, scripts/screenshot/machine_settings_camera.py reaches into CameraController privates (_raw_image_data, _image_data, _running at :88-92) and into CameraPreferencesPage.camera_list_editor.list_box (:111) — any rename inside the controller or the prefs page breaks the screenshot pipeline with no test to catch it. Also note tests/camera/visual_test_camera_scaling.py is named so pytest will not collect it; it is a hand-run script, so treating it as covered test surface would be wrong.

### External dependencies

- cv2 / opencv_python — declared in requirements.txt:8 (unpinned) and debian/control:28 (python3-opencv). NOT camera-only: also imported by rayforge/image/tracing.py:7, rayforge/image/util/srgb.py:9, tests/image/test_hull.py:1 and scripts/win/windows_import_trace.py:20. Removing the camera area would NOT let you drop OpenCV.
- cv2.aruco (the ChArUco submodule) — this IS camera-only. Every use is in rayforge/camera/calibration/charuco.py (lines 16, 34, 50, 53, 59, 60, 61, 65, 66, 214). Nothing else in the repo touches cv2.aruco.
- numpy — requirements.txt:7, pixi.toml:30, debian/control (python3-numpy). Used repo-wide; nowhere near camera-only.
- blinker — requirements.txt:4. Signals used by Camera, CameraController, CameraManager, and several camera widgets; used repo-wide.
- PyGObject / gi (Gtk, Adw, Gdk, GdkPixbuf, GLib, Graphene, Pango) — all camera UI. Repo-wide.
- pycairo — rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/camera_image.py:6 uses cairo.ImageSurface. Repo-wide.
- multiprocessing (stdlib, spawn context) — rayforge/camera/controller.py:2, used only for the isolated device-probe pool. No other subsystem spawns a pool this way, but it is stdlib so nothing to remove.
- snap 'camera' interface plug — snap/snapcraft.yaml:21. This one IS camera-only and would be droppable.
- No opencv/cv2 entry exists in pyproject.toml (deps are dynamic from requirements.txt, pyproject.toml line ~38) or in pixi.toml — I grepped both; pixi.toml only pins numpy==2.5.2. debian/requirements-bundle.txt has no opencv/numpy lines.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (9)

- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_maps.py:479 — the ONLY appearance of the word 'camera' anywhere under rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/. It is a Ruida parameter label string, `0x0193: ("Color Mark Camera Distance", 0)`. Zero code coupling: the Ruida driver imports nothing from rayforge/camera or rayforge/ui_gtk/camera. Verified by grepping the whole ruida directory.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py — verified ZERO camera references. The jog panel and the camera subsystem do not touch.
- The pipeline job path (intent_builder / build_rd_bytes / send_job / .rd export) — verified ZERO camera references; no file under rayforge/camera or rayforge/ui_gtk/camera imports anything from the pipeline, and no pipeline file imports camera.
- docs/design/ layout tokens (Swift Cut): 13 camera UI files import SPACE_* / icon_button from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py — alignment_dialog.py:8, alignment_widget.py:20, camera_preferences_page.py:10, image_settings_dialog.py:7, image_settings_widget.py:13, lens_calibration_dialog.py:7, point_bubble_widget.py:8, properties_widget.py:9, selection_dialog.py:11, wizard/capture_page.py:11, wizard/card_page.py:18, wizard/lens_calibration_settings_page.py:8, wizard/wizard.py:20. These are one-way consumers: the layout module does not know about camera.
- docs/design/audit/AUDIT.md:238-239 — cites camera/wizard/card_page.py:104 and :114 (Width / Height rows) as layout-audit anchors.
- docs/design/audit/AUDIT.md:295-296 — cites camera/alignment_widget.py:281, camera/camera_preferences_page.py:62, camera/point_bubble_widget.py:112-115 in the hardcoded-spacing audit table.
- docs/design/audit/{,after/}machine-settings-camera-{dark,light}-{1280,1920}.png — 8 committed audit screenshots of the camera settings page. Regenerated by scripts/screenshot/machine_settings_camera.py via scripts/screenshot/ui_audit.py:78.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py — camera fields live directly on the protected-adjacent Machine model: self.cameras at :152, add_camera :1095-1098, remove_camera :1100-1103, _on_camera_changed :1105, to_dict 'cameras' key :1504, from_dict rehydration :1797-1799.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine_panel.py:127-141 — MachinePanel._reproject_cameras rewrites camera.image_to_world when bed orientation or axis_extents change. Called from set_orientation (:100) and _on_machine_changed (:124). This is the deepest non-UI coupling and it is bidirectional in spirit: panel logic reads and writes camera calibration.

<a id="update-telemetry"></a>

## update-telemetry

### Summary

Three loosely-related startup concerns share exactly one trigger point: MainWindow._trigger_startup_tasks (rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:541-563), a one-shot "map" handler. (a) The app update checker is rayforge/updater.py (AppUpdateChecker), instantiated at mainwindow.py:175, wired to the toast handler _on_editor_notification at :176, and fired at :563; it does one aiohttp GET to GITHUB_RELEASES_API and, if is_newer_version() says so, raises a persistent toast with a "Download" button that opens DOWNLOAD_URL in a webbrowser. It is gated by config.check_for_app_updates. (b) Telemetry is rayforge/usage.py (a process-wide singleton UsageTracker + get_usage_tracker()), which POSTs Umami page-view events over stdlib urllib on a daemon thread; it is disabled by default and only set_enabled(True) in two places (mainwindow.py:551 on saved consent, general_preferences_page.py:514 and usage_consent_dialog.py:51 on toggle/response). Its track_page_view() is called from 20+ sites, most importantly the two generic mixins TrackedPreferencesPage (rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/preferences_page.py) and PatchedDialogWindow/PatchedMessageDialog (rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/patched_dialog_window.py), which nearly every settings page and dialog in the app inherits from. (c) The only dialog the app shows on launch is UsageConsentDialog (mainwindow.py:556), presented when config.usage_consent_date is None (never asked); StartupBehavior in rayforge/core/config.py is a separate, non-dialog startup concern consumed only by App._load_startup_files (rayforge/app.py:415-450) and the settings combo row. All persisted keys (check_for_app_updates, usage_consent_date, startup_behavior, startup_project_path) live in the shared Config class in rayforge/core/config.py alongside unrelated keys.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Three very different risk tiers here. TIER 1 — safe to remove cleanly: rayforge/updater.py plus tests/test_updater.py. Its entire production surface is five lines in mainwindow.py (32, 175-178, 563) and one config key. Deleting it orphans const.py's GITHUB_RELEASES_API and DOWNLOAD_URL (nothing else references them, verified), leaves is_newer_version still needed by addon_mgr, and leaves aiohttp still needed by five other modules. Order: remove the mainwindow wiring first, then updater.py + its test, then the const.py constants, then the check_for_app_updates key + its settings row + the .pot/.po msgids and website/docs/ui/settings.md:36-38. TIER 2 — removable but with a long tail: rayforge/usage.py. get_usage_tracker() has 20 call sites in 12 files; the dangerous ones are the two mixins. If you delete usage.py without first stripping rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/preferences_page.py and rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/patched_dialog_window.py, you break the import for ~24 preference pages and ~23 dialogs at once — including the step-settings pages and the machine settings dialog. Safe order: (1) gut _on_map/_track_view in the two mixins (keep the classes — the Adw.Window focus fix and the PreferencesPage base are load-bearing and unrelated to telemetry), (2) remove the ~14 direct track_page_view calls in doceditor/*_cmd.py, ui_gtk/project_cmd.py, view_mode_cmd.py, console.py, mainwindow.py:1865 and the sketcher addon, (3) delete usage.py + usage_consent_dialog.py + the mainwindow consent branch (549-557), (4) drop UMAMI_URL/UMAMI_WEBSITE_ID from rayforge/config.py:76-77, (5) drop usage_consent_date + set_usage_consent + has_consented_tracking + has_declined_tracking from core/config.py and its to_dict/from_dict lines, (6) drop the Privacy group from general_preferences_page.py:338-370 and on_usage_consent_changed, (7) retire website/docs/general-info/usage-tracking.md, its sidebars.js:120 entry, its 6 translations, and the settings.md Privacy section + 6 translations. Note the sketcher builtin addon imports rayforge.usage by absolute path, so an addon breaks if step 2 is skipped. TIER 3 — do NOT remove: StartupBehavior. It is not telemetry; it is the 'reopen my last project' feature, consumed by rayforge/app.py:415-450, and deleting it changes launch behavior for existing users whose config says last_project. It shares core/config.py, general_preferences_page.py and the same startup window with the other two only by accident. Cross-cutting risks: (a) the .pot msgid 'Rayforge {version} is available.' is already stale versus updater.py's '{app} {version} is available.', so the update toast is currently untranslated — any string edit needs a catalog regeneration, and three more updater strings still say 'Rayforge' rather than 'Swift Cut'; (b) rayforge/usage.py has zero test coverage anywhere in tests/, so a refactor there has no safety net (tests/test_updater.py covers only the updater); (c) has_consented_tracking's hard-coded 2026-02-24 policy date (core/config.py:214) means a pre-2026 consent re-triggers the startup dialog — expected behavior, but it makes 'did the dialog show?' non-obvious when testing.

### External dependencies

- aiohttp — used by rayforge/updater.py:7 for the GitHub releases GET. ALSO used elsewhere in the repo, so it cannot be dropped from dependencies: rayforge/core/ai/openai_provider.py:6, rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_network.py:13, rayforge/machine/driver/octoprint/octoprint_driver.py:13, rayforge/machine/transport/http.py:3, scripts/media/update_supporters.py:9. It is a declared Debian dep at debian/control:29 (python3-aiohttp).
- blinker — Signal used for AppUpdateChecker.notification_requested (rayforge/updater.py:8). Used pervasively across the codebase (Config.changed, DocEditor, TaskManager, etc.); not removable.
- urllib.request / urllib.error (stdlib) — the telemetry transport in rayforge/usage.py:6-7. Stdlib, nothing to uninstall. Notably the tracker does NOT use aiohttp; it spawns its own daemon thread (usage.py:154-155).
- threading, uuid, locale, platform, json (stdlib) — rayforge/usage.py for the singleton lock, session id, language, OS string and payload encoding.
- webbrowser (stdlib) — rayforge/updater.py:3, opens DOWNLOAD_URL from the update toast's action button.
- gi.repository Adw/Gtk — the consent dialog and the settings rows. Used app-wide.
- packaging (via rayforge/shared/util/versioning.py) — indirect dependency of the update check through is_newer_version; that module is ALSO used by rayforge/addon_mgr/, so it is not exclusive to this area. I did not open versioning.py's own imports to confirm which parsing library it uses.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (9)

- rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/usage_consent_dialog.py:7 and :33 — imports and uses the layout token SPACE_GROUP from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py (Swift Cut layout token system, docs/design/). Any edit to this dialog must keep the token, not a literal.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/general_preferences_page.py:17 and :369 — imports and uses the layout token SPACE_CONTROL from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/general_preferences_page.py:184-230 — the SAME FILE that holds the update/telemetry/startup rows also holds the speed unit ComboRow (mm/s units, protected) plus length and acceleration unit rows and their handlers at :443-470. Edits here are not isolated from the protected units UI.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:1865 — get_usage_tracker().track_page_view('/bottom-panel/open', ...) sits inside on_toggle_bottom_panel_state_change, i.e. inside the bottom/jog panel show-hide behavior. Touching it means touching jog-panel code.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/console.py:531 — get_usage_tracker().track_page_view('/console/send', 'Console Send') is inside _send_commands, immediately before command_submitted.send dispatches raw commands to the machine. Adjacent to the machine send path.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/preferences_page.py — TrackedPreferencesPage is the base class of rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/step_settings/pages/base.py:27 (StepSettingsPage), i.e. the layer/step settings system inherits from the telemetry mixin.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/patched_dialog_window.py — PatchedDialogWindow is the base of rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/step_settings/dialog.py:34 (step settings dialog, skip_usage_tracking=True) and rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py:57. The focus-bug fix in this file is load-bearing for those protected UIs.
- rayforge/core/config.py — the four keys owned by this area (check_for_app_updates L79, usage_consent_date L82, startup_behavior L67, startup_project_path L69-70) live in the same class and the same to_dict/from_dict as unit_preferences (mm/s), machine, canvas_view and ops_color_mode. Any change to to_dict (L225-250) / from_dict (L252-330) risks the protected units and machine config round-trip.
- tests/config/config.yaml — the fixture that carries usage_consent_date/startup_behavior/check_for_app_updates also carries 'speed: mm/min' (L38) and the machine UUID; it is loaded through tests/conftest.py:256/:280/:478.

<a id="sim3d"></a>

## sim3d — 3D preview / simulation subsystem

### Summary

The 3D preview is a **page inside the main window's `view_stack`**, not a separate window: `MainWindow._create_canvas3d()` (rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:1392-1427) builds a `Canvas3D` (a `Gtk.GLArea`), wraps it in a `Gtk.Overlay` with a `VisibilityOverlay` and a `TimeEstimateOverlay`, appends a `PlaybackOverlay` bar below it, and registers the whole box as `view_stack` child `"3d"` next to the 2D `"2d"` page. It is reached by the `win.show_3d_view` stateful action (F12) from the View menu and the toolbar toggle button; `ViewModeCmd.toggle_3d_view` just flips `view_stack` to `"3d"`. The subsystem splits cleanly in two: `rayforge/simulator/**` is headless/no-GL (numpy only) — `OpPlayer`/`MachineState` playback state machine plus `scene3d/` which compiles a `JobArtifact` into a `CompiledSceneArtifact` of GPU-ready vertex/texture/overlay arrays — and `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/**` is the PyOpenGL layer (camera, shaders, ~13 renderers, chunked GPU upload, per-frame `RenderContext`). It is a pure **consumer** of the protected job pipeline: it listens to `pipeline.job_generation_finished` / `processing_state_changed` and reads `JobArtifact.preview_ops`; it never feeds the `send_job` / `build_rd_bytes` / `.rd` export path. The whole GL half is guarded by `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/__init__.py`'s `initialize()` / `initialized` flag (and `RAYFORGE_DISABLE_3D`), which four unrelated modules import to decide whether to offer 3D features.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Deleting rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/** and rayforge/simulator/** wholesale would break FOUR things that have nothing to do with the 3D view.

(1) The availability flag. Five modules import `initialized` from rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/__init__.py: app.py:563, mainwindow.py:66, toolbar.py:13, view_mode_cmd.py:35, model_selection_dialog.py:13. Each is an immediate ImportError.

(2) The machine-settings model UI. rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/head_preferences_page.py:24 and rotary_module_page.py:24 import get_model_extent from sim3d/renderer/model_renderer.py; rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/model_preview_widget.py:14-18 imports FIVE sim3d modules (camera, gl_state.render_pass, renderer.base.BaseRenderer, model_renderer._load_mesh_data, shader). model_preview_widget is reached from model_selection_dialog.py:120, which both machine-settings pages open. None of this is the 3D preview.

(3) machine_state.py is not sim3d property. rayforge/machine/assembly.py:16 and rayforge/machine/kinematics.py:13 type against it, and tests/machine/test_assembly.py, tests/machine/models/test_kinematics.py, tests/machine/models/test_machine_assembly.py construct it at runtime.

(4) tests/machine/test_kinematic_mapping.py:23 imports OpPlayer and SnapshotBuilder as the reference implementation for rotary-mapping assertions (lines 397, 425).

SAFE REMOVAL ORDER, if this is ever pursued:
Step 1 — sever the non-3D consumers first. Move get_model_extent() and _load_mesh_data() (model_renderer.py:58,128) plus the minimal Camera/BaseRenderer/render_pass/Shader surface that model_preview_widget.py needs OUT of sim3d into a neutral home (e.g. rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/), keeping trimesh and PyOpenGL alive for the settings preview.
Step 2 — decide machine_state.py's home. Either leave rayforge/simulator/machine_state.py in place, or relocate it under rayforge/machine/ and update assembly.py:16, kinematics.py:13 and the three tests/machine/ imports. It must NOT be deleted.
Step 3 — remove the UI surface: the show_3d_view + seven view actions (actions.py:58-67, 259-262, 310-321), the menu section (main_menu.py:92-106), the toolbar button (toolbar.py:79-89), ViewModeCmd.toggle_3d_view/set_view/toggle_perspective, and mainwindow's canvas3d wiring (mainwindow.py:65-69, 144-145, 369-370, 802-812, 826-842, 856-860, 881-925, 1392-1427, 1808-1814). Watch mainwindow.py:1364 (`is_3d_visible`) in refresh_previews() — it must be edited without disturbing the G-code branch or assemble_job_in_background.
Step 4 — remove the OpPlayer + scene3d + GL layer and their tests. Only then does PyOpenGL become unreferenced (assuming step 1 relocated the settings preview's needs, otherwise PyOpenGL must stay).
Step 5 — DO NOT touch rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py:386-408 or rayforge/pipeline/artifact/job.py. mapped_ops/preview_ops would become dead, but that path is protected; leave it and note the dead field.

WHAT STAYS REGARDLESS: numpy, blinker, raygeo, ColorLutProvider (shared with theme_service.py:22), TimeEstimateOverlay (rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/time_estimate_overlay.py — used on BOTH the 2D surface at mainwindow.py:356 and the 3D page at 1417), VisibilityOverlay (shared, mainwindow.py:345 and 1404), and the whole rayforge/machine/ kinematics stack.

BIGGEST SURPRISE, and the answer to the removal question you asked: the OpPlayer/simulator serves nothing outside the 3D view. The elapsed/estimated time shown in the 2D panel comes from a completely different source — pipeline.job_time_updated carrying ops.estimate_time() (rayforge/machine/cmd.py:206) into mainwindow.py:2317. No 2D panel reads OpPlayer. The real entanglement is not playback at all; it is trimesh mesh loading sitting inside sim3d/renderer/model_renderer.py.

### External dependencies

- PyOpenGL==3.1.10 and PyOpenGL_accelerate==3.1.10 (requirements.txt:15-16, pixi.toml:32-33) — used ONLY inside rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/** plus rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/model_preview_widget.py. Verified by `grep -rln 'from OpenGL|import OpenGL' rayforge/ tests/ scripts/`: 20 hits in sim3d, 1 in model_preview_widget.py, 3 in tests/ui_gtk/sim3d/. Packaged as python3-opengl in debian/control:30 and the opengl plug in snap/snapcraft.yaml:20.
- trimesh==5.0.0 (requirements.txt:24, pixi.toml:56) — the single import site in the whole repo is rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/model_renderer.py:10, plus tests/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/test_model_renderer.py. BUT model_renderer's get_model_extent()/_load_mesh_data() are consumed by three NON-3D-view modules (head_preferences_page.py, rotary_module_page.py, model_preview_widget.py), so trimesh is not droppable by removing the 3D view alone.
- numpy==2.5.2 (requirements.txt:9) — used pervasively here (rayforge/simulator/scene3d/*, camera.py, camera_controller.py, gl_state.py, gl_utils.py, every renderer) but it is a repo-wide dependency (also debian/control:12,27, pixi.toml:30). NOT sim3d-only.
- blinker==1.9.0 — Signal used by op_player.py (layer_changed), playback_overlay.py (step_changed), scene_presenter.py. Repo-wide dependency, not sim3d-only.
- raygeo==1.38.3 — Ops/Axis/CommandType consumed by op_player.py, machine_state.py, scene_compiler.py (rasterize_scanlines), viewport.py. Repo-wide, not sim3d-only.
- PyGObject / GTK4 / Adw — the Gtk.GLArea host widget. Repo-wide.
- NOT USED HERE (checked and absent): pyglet, moderngl, PyOpenGL's GLUT, scipy in sim3d, Pillow, freetype, pygltflib. The 3D stack is raw PyOpenGL + numpy + trimesh only; text rendering builds its own atlas via Pango/cairo in text_renderer.py rather than a font library.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (12)

- rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py:386-408 — mapped_ops is produced here (KinematicMapping.apply_to_job_ops on a copy of the job Ops) and stored on JobArtifact. This is inside the PROTECTED job assembly path. Its ONLY consumers anywhere are rayforge/simulator/scene3d/scene_compiler_runner.py:41 and rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/scene_presenter.py:436, both via JobArtifact.preview_ops. Report only — do not touch.
- rayforge/pipeline/artifact/job.py:44-78 — the JobArtifact docstring and the preview_ops property exist specifically for the 3D preview/OpPlayer ("Suitable for 3D preview and playback (scene compiler, OpPlayer). Not suitable for G-code encoding"). PROTECTED file.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/scene_presenter.py:108-126 — subscribes to pipeline.processing_state_changed and pipeline.job_generation_finished (signals defined at rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py:89 and emitted at pipeline.py:363,417). Read-only consumption of the protected job path.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/canvas3d.py:11 and :196-197 — imports rayforge.pipeline.pipeline.Pipeline and reads pipeline.last_completed_handle. Read-only.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:1356-1383 refresh_previews() — a SHARED trigger: it fires the protected assemble_job_in_background() path when EITHER the G-code panel OR the 3D page is visible (is_3d_visible = view_stack.get_visible_child_name() == '3d' at mainwindow.py:1364). The 3D view is one of two things keeping this alive.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:788-812 — bidirectional sync between the G-code viewer and the 3D playback slider: _on_gcode_line_activated calls self._canvas3d_playback.set_playback_position(op_index), and _on_3d_playback_step_changed calls bottom_panel.gcode_viewer.highlight_op(). NOTE a latent pre-existing bug: self._canvas3d_playback is only assigned in _create_canvas3d (mainwindow.py:1412) and is NOT initialized to None in __init__ (mainwindow.py:138-146), so mainwindow.py:802 would raise AttributeError when canvas3d_initialized is False. Reporting, not fixing.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/playback_overlay.py:8-9 and :87 — imports SPACE_CONTROL from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py and adds the .sc-overlay class. Swift Cut layout tokens are PROTECTED (docs/design/swift-cut-layout.md:120,237).
- rayforge/ui_gtk/theme.py:305-316 — the shared _LAYOUT CSS carries a comment explicitly written to protect the 3D playback speed button ("1x") from being crushed by a bare `.sc-overlay button` rule. Any change to that CSS block affects the playback bar and vice versa. PROTECTED (docs/design/).
- docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:307 — references the live-progress behaviour "which the 3D playback view already drives". PROTECTED doc; the 3D playback is cited as the precedent.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/doc_signals.py:74,80,149 — connects to doc.active_layer_changed. The layer/step system is PROTECTED; this is a read-only subscriber.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/render_context/kinematics.py:134 — calls Assembly.head_positions(state, wcs_offset=wcs) from rayforge/machine/assembly.py:444. Also scene_presenter.py:20-24 imports build_layer_assembly / resolve_layer_rotary from rayforge/machine/kinematic_mapping.py (whose docstrings at lines 67 and 245 name OpPlayer as a co-consumer). Read-only.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_simulator.py and ruida_server.py — NAME COLLISION ONLY. These are the Ruida controller network simulator (PROTECTED) and share nothing with rayforge/simulator/ or the 3D view. Confirmed no import edges in either direction.

### Entry points (16)

| Where | What |
|---|---|
| `rayforge/ui_gtk/main_menu.py:93` | View menu > "3D View" item, bound to win.show_3d_view. The primary discoverable entry point. |

<details><summary>Module notes (34)</summary>

- `rayforge/simulator/__init__.py` - Confirmed 0 bytes via wc -c. No re-exports; every consumer imports the submodule directly.
- `rayforge/simulator/machine_state.py` - NOT sim3d-only. It is the state object that rayforge/machine/assembly.py's head_positions()/chuck_angles() and rayforge/machine/kinematics.py's solver take as their argument, and three tests under tests/machine/ construct it at runtime. Deleting it breaks the machine kinematics domain and its tests, even though the only PRODUCTION caller of head_positions()/chuck_angles() is sim3d (rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/render_context/kinematics.py:134 and rayforge/machine/assembly.py:318 internally).
- `rayforge/simulator/op_player.py` - ANSWERS THE REMOVAL QUESTION: OpPlayer serves NOTHING outside the 3D view. Grepped every construction site — only scene_presenter.py:274 plus tests. Its layer_changed signal has exactly one subscriber (scene_presenter.py:300). The wall-clock time estimate shown in the 2D panel does NOT come from OpPlayer: TimeEstimateOverlay is fed from pipeline.job_time_updated -> mainwindow.py:2317, and the number originates in ops.estimate_time() (rayforge/machine/cmd.py:206) / JobArtifact.time_estimate. Caveat: tests/machine/test_kinematic_mapping.py:397,425 uses OpPlayer as the reference implementation for rotary-mapping tests, so that test file would need rework.
- `rayforge/simulator/scene3d/__init__.py` - Every inbound reference is inside rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/.
- `rayforge/simulator/scene3d/compiled_scene.py` - Dependency direction is one-way: scene3d depends on rayforge/pipeline/artifact/{base,handle}.py, the pipeline does NOT know about CompiledSceneArtifact. The handle registry (rayforge/pipeline/artifact/handle.py:66 create_handle_from_dict) is name-keyed via a metaclass-style _handle_registry, so an unregistered class only fails if a dict naming it is deserialized — no hardcoded pipeline reference to remove.
- `rayforge/simulator/scene3d/cylinder_compiler.py` - No test file of its own; exercised through tests/simulator/test_scene_compiler.py.
- `rayforge/simulator/scene3d/scene_compiler_runner.py` - scene_compiler_runner.py:41 is the ONLY production consumer of JobArtifact.preview_ops besides scene_presenter.py:436.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/__init__.py` - This is the widest blast radius in the whole area: five modules outside sim3d import `initialized` to enable/disable UI. Note model_selection_dialog.py:111 uses it for the machine-settings model picker, which is NOT the 3D view.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/canvas3d.py` - canvas3d.py:11 imports rayforge.pipeline.pipeline.Pipeline and canvas3d.py:197 reads pipeline.last_completed_handle — read-only coupling into the protected job path.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/scene_presenter.py` - The single point where sim3d touches the pipeline (scene_presenter.py:108-126) and the single OpPlayer construction site (scene_presenter.py:274).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/playback_overlay.py` - Talks to OpPlayer only through the PlaybackPlayer Protocol (playback_overlay.py:15-33), so it is decoupled from rayforge/simulator. It imports SPACE_CONTROL from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py and adds the .sc-overlay CSS class — a PROTECTED layout-token touchpoint. rayforge/ui_gtk/theme.py:313 carries a comment explicitly protecting the 3D playback speed button from a .sc-overlay rule.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/camera.py` - Shared with the non-3D-view model preview widget.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/doc_signals.py` - doc_signals.py:74 connects to doc.active_layer_changed — a read-only subscription to the layer system (PROTECTED).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/viewport.py` - mainwindow.py imports ViewportConfig as a type for the _create_canvas3d(context, viewport) signature.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/gl_state.py` - Shared outside the 3D view by the settings model preview.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/theme_resolver.py` - Consumes rayforge/core/color.py ColorSet and the shared theme service; owns no colour source of its own.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/render_context/kinematics.py` - This is the ONLY production caller of Assembly.head_positions() (kinematics.py:134). rayforge/machine/kinematics.py's KinematicsSolver.head_positions/chuck_angles has no other production caller — grep confirmed only assembly.py:318 internal use plus tests/machine/.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/__init__.py` - Confirmed 0 lines via wc -l.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/base.py` - Shared outside the 3D view: model_preview_widget subclasses it for the settings model preview.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/background_renderer.py` - No dedicated test file.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/laser_beam_renderer.py` - laser_beam_renderer.py:85-90 reads ctx.playback.op_player directly. No dedicated test file.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/model_renderer.py` - MOST ENTANGLED FILE IN THE AREA. It is the sole trimesh consumer in the whole repo, yet three machine-settings UI modules that have nothing to do with the 3D view import from it. Removing sim3d without extracting get_model_extent()/_load_mesh_data() breaks Machine Settings > Head and > Rotary Module.

<a id="drivers"></a>

## drivers

### Summary

The driver layer lives entirely under `rayforge/machine/driver/` (28 Python files, ~15.9k lines). `driver.py` defines the abstract `Driver` ABC plus the shared value types (`DeviceState`, `DeviceStatus`, `DeviceError`, `Pos`, `PWMParams`, `pwm_varset`, `DriverMaturity`/`DRIVER_MATURITY_LABELS`, `DriverPrecheckError`, `DriverSetupError`, `DeviceConnectionError`, `ResourceBusyError`, `acceleration_run_up_mm`). Discovery is NOT a plugin system: `rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py` statically imports all nine concrete classes and builds `drivers` (a list, in import order) and `driver_by_classname` (a dict) by scanning module `locals()` with `isdriver()`; `get_driver_cls(classname, default=NoDeviceDriver)` is the single lookup choke point and `register_driver()` is the only extension hook (used solely by `tests/machine/models/test_machine.py:52`). A `Machine` stores only the driver's *class name string* in `Machine.driver_name` plus `driver_args`/`driver_config` (`rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:131-133`), and `MachineController.rebuild_driver()` (`rayforge/machine/models/controller.py:173-202`) resolves that string through `get_driver_cls`, runs `precheck(**driver_args)`, instantiates, calls `setup()`, and rewires signals. The user picks a driver in two places: the Add-Machine wizard's `ControllerPage` (a tile grid built from the `drivers` list) and the Machine Settings dialog's General page `Adw.ComboRow` (also built from `drivers`, indexed by `drivers.index(driver_cls)`). Verified live registry order: NoDeviceDriver, GrblNetworkDriver, GrblSerialDriver, GrblSerialSimpleDriver, GrblTelnetDriver, MarlinSerialDriver, OctoPrintDriver, RuidaDriver, SmoothieDriver.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

The discovery mechanism is a single static file, so a "keep only Ruida + dummy" removal is mechanically shallow but has a wide blast radius in resources, tests and one script.

WHAT ACTUALLY BREAKS AT IMPORT TIME (hard failures):
1. `rayforge/machine/driver/smoothie.py:26` does `from .grbl.grbl_util import parse_state`. Smoothie must be removed before or together with the grbl package.
2. `rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_telnet.py:15` subclasses `GrblSerialDriver`; grbl_telnet must go before grbl_serial.
3. `scripts/screenshot/machine_settings_device.py:14` imports `get_grbl_setting_varsets` from `rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_util.py`. This script dies if the grbl package is deleted.
4. Twelve test files import the removed classes directly and would fail collection: tests/machine/driver/grbl/{test_grbl_serial_driver,test_grbl_serial_driver_stress,test_grbl_serial_simple_driver,test_grbl_probe,test_grbl_util}.py, tests/machine/driver/marlin/{test_marlin_serial_driver,test_marlin_probe,test_marlin_util}.py, tests/machine/driver/octoprint/test_octoprint_driver.py, tests/machine/driver/test_smoothie_driver.py — plus two model tests that import them but are NOT driver tests: tests/machine/models/test_machine.py:23-25 (uses GrblNetworkDriver/GrblSerialDriver/SmoothieDriver at L1023-1221) and tests/machine/models/test_profile_driver_assignment.py:8-9.

WHAT DEGRADES SILENTLY (no crash, wrong behavior):
5. `get_driver_cls` (rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:37-38) returns NoDeviceDriver for any unknown name. So the 25 bundled profiles under rayforge/resources/devices/ that name GrblSerialDriver (21), GrblNetworkDriver (2) and SmoothieDriver (2) would still load but silently become "no driver" machines; the wizard review page would show "Unknown driver: GrblSerialDriver" (review_page.py:160). Those 25 device directories would need deleting or repointing.
6. `rayforge/machine/device/lightburn_importer.py:28-29` maps LightBurn "Serial"/"Network" to Grbl class names — imported .lbdev files would silently lose their driver. Its tests (tests/machine/device/test_lbdev_importer.py, ~20 assertions) and tests/machine/device/test_device_profile.py (~15 assertions on "GrblSerialDriver"/"GrblNetworkDriver") would fail.
7. `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/controller_page.py:26-33` `_DRIVER_ICONS` has a fallback (`_FALLBACK_ICON`), so stale keys are harmless dead entries.
8. tests/ui_gtk/machine/test_unified_wizard.py builds profiles with driver="GrblSerialDriver" (L121, 156, 263, 647, 666, 685) and "OctoPrintDriver" (L399) — assertions would drift.

ORPHANED AFTER REMOVAL (would become dead, in the transport layer which is NOT part of my area):
- rayforge/machine/transport/websocket.py and http.py (only consumer was grbl_network.py:21)
- rayforge/machine/transport/grbl.py `GrblSerialTransport` (only grbl_serial.py:34, grbl_telnet.py:8)
- rayforge/machine/transport/telnet.py `TelnetTransport` (only smoothie.py:17, grbl_telnet.py:7)
- rayforge/machine/transport/serial.py CANNOT be dropped: rayforge/ui_gtk/varset/adapter/combo.py:11 still calls SerialTransport.list_ports() for the SerialPortVar picker.
- `websockets` becomes an unused dependency (requirements.txt, pixi.toml:58, debian/control:29). `pyserial` and `aiohttp` do NOT (see external_dependencies).

SAFE REMOVAL ORDER: (a) delete the 12 driver test files and fix the 5 model/device/ui test files that import removed classes; (b) delete scripts/screenshot/machine_settings_device.py's grbl import (or the script) and fix scripts/screenshot/config_wizard.py:53; (c) delete/repoint the 25 non-Ruida device.yaml profile directories; (d) fix lightburn_importer.py:27-36 _DRIVER_MAP and its tests; (e) remove the imports and __all__ entries from rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:12-21 and :50-59; (f) delete smoothie.py, then octoprint/, then marlin/, then grbl/ (telnet before serial); (g) optionally prune the orphaned transports and the `websockets` pin; (h) leave rayforge/machine/driver/driver.py, dummy.py and all of ruida/ untouched.

WHAT MUST NOT CHANGE: driver.py (Ruida inherits from it and ruida_encoder.py:23 imports acceleration_run_up_mm), dummy.py (nine isinstance gates in the UI plus the get_driver_cls fallback), and the get_driver_cls / create_encoder contract used by the protected job path (intent_builder.py:976-982, machine/cmd.py:754-759).

### External dependencies

- pyserial (`import serial.serialutil` at rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_serial.py:12 and rayforge/machine/driver/marlin/marlin_serial.py:12). ALSO used outside the driver layer by rayforge/machine/transport/serial.py:9 — and that transport is reached from the UI by rayforge/ui_gtk/varset/adapter/combo.py:11 (the serial-port picker calls SerialTransport.list_ports()). So pyserial cannot be dropped just by removing GRBL/Marlin.
- aiohttp (rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_network.py:13, rayforge/machine/driver/octoprint/octoprint_driver.py:13). ALSO used by rayforge/core/ai/openai_provider.py:6, rayforge/machine/transport/http.py:3, rayforge/updater.py:7 and scripts/media/update_supporters.py:9 — stays regardless.
- websockets (rayforge/machine/transport/websocket.py:4 only). Its ONLY consumer is rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_network.py:21. Removing GrblNetworkDriver orphans both the transport and this dependency (requirements.txt, pixi.toml:58, debian/control:29).
- asyncudp (rayforge/machine/transport/udp.py:5). Consumers: PROTECTED rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:22 and ruida_client.py:111, plus tests/machine/transport/test_udp_transport.py:3. Stays.
- blinker (Signal) — used by driver.py:13, grbl_probe.py:11, marlin_probe.py:11, ruida_client.py:13, ruida_transport.py:10, ruida_simulator.py:23. Used app-wide.
- raygeo (raygeo.ops.axis.Axis in driver.py:14, dummy.py:12, octoprint_driver.py:14; raygeo.ops / geo.types in ruida_encoder.py:13-16). Used app-wide.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (16)

- rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:20 — `from .ruida import RuidaDriver`; the registry is the only thing that makes RuidaDriver selectable. Line 58 lists it in __all__.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_encoder.py:23 — `from ..driver import acceleration_run_up_mm`: the PROTECTED encoder reaches UP into the base driver module, so driver.py must keep that function.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:23 — imports DeviceStatus/Driver/Pos/etc. from ../driver.py. Base class is load-bearing for Ruida.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:1678 — `if ma.driver_name == "RuidaDriver" and ma.active_wcs == "MACHINE":` a one-shot deserialization migration that rewrites the reference point to REF0. Hard-coded Ruida class-name string outside the driver package.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:1654-1657 — dialect_uid defaulting keys off `driver_cls.uses_gcode`, which is False only for RuidaDriver.
- rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:375-389 and :405-430 — `driver_uses_gcode` gates whether a device profile REQUIRES a dialect file; the non-G-code branch exists for Ruida.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py:298-311 — hides the G-code page for non-G-code (Ruida) drivers.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/controller_page.py:28 — `"RuidaDriver": "network-wired-symbolic"` icon-map entry.
- rayforge/machine/device/lightburn_importer.py:30 — `"Ruida": "RuidaDriver"` in _DRIVER_MAP.
- rayforge/doceditor/file_cmd.py:49 — `from ..machine.driver.ruida.ruida_encoder import export_rd`; used at file_cmd.py:1038 (the PROTECTED .rd export job path). Reached from rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:2017 and actions.py:249.
- rayforge/image/ruida/parser.py:3 — the .rd FILE IMPORTER imports encode/decode helpers from rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_util.py. A cross-subsystem read into protected code.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:72-73 and :976-982 — PROTECTED job path: resolves get_driver_cls(machine.driver_name) then driver_cls.create_encoder(machine) to build the encoder callable. Depends on the registry contract, not on any specific driver.
- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:19-20 and :754-759 (_create_driver_encoder) — same registry contract on the send_job / frame path; cmd.py:369 send_job, cmd.py:190/197 driver.run().
- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:600-625 — jog_key_down / jog_key_up / release_all_jog_keys delegate straight to the driver; this is the jog-panel hold behavior (driver.py:663-708 can_hold_jog/jog_key_down/jog_key_up).
- rayforge/resources/devices/monport-60w-co2/device.yaml:6, rayforge/resources/devices/omtech-polar/device.yaml:6, rayforge/resources/devices/thunder-laser-nova35/device.yaml:6 — the three bundled profiles that select RuidaDriver.
- tests/machine/driver/ruida/** — 19 protected test/support files; also tests/doceditor/test_file_cmd.py:1127 and :1185 exercise the Ruida export path.

- `rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py` - Only two symbols are enumerated rather than looked up: `drivers` (the ordered list) is imported by general_preferences_page.py:6 and controller_page.py:17. This file is load-bearing for ALL drivers including Ruida — it can never be removed.

<a id="gcode"></a>

## gcode

### Summary

The GCODE subsystem has four layers: (1) the encoder package `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/` — `base.py` (driver-agnostic `OpsEncoder`/`EncodedOutput`/`MachineCodeOpMap`), `gcode.py` (`GcodeEncoder`, a thin wrapper over raygeo's Rust encoder), `rust_helpers.py` (`dialect_to_spec`/`build_encode_context`) and `context.py` (`GcodeContext`, the template-variable namespace); (2) the dialect model `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/**` plus `dialect_manager.py`, which is a first-class field on every `Machine` (`Machine.__init__` sets `dialect_uid = "grbl"` and `machine.py:33` imports `GcodeDialect` at runtime); (3) the dialect selection UI (`settings_dialog` -> `gcode_settings_page` -> `dialect_list` -> `dialect_editor` -> `template_selector`) and the macro/hook G-code editor (`gcode_editor.py` used by `hook_list.py`/`macro_list.py`); (4) the shared text widgets `rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/**` (`GcodeEditor`/`GcodeHighlighter`/`GcodeViewer`) plus the driver-agnostic `Console`. ANSWER TO THE CRITICAL QUESTION: YES — the Ruida job path imports gcode/dialect modules, in four concrete places, but never *executes* any G-code encoding. `intent_builder.py:76` imports `GRBL_DIALECT` at module level and `intent_builder.py:814-815` evaluates `machine.dialect` + `_is_grbl(dialect)` on every job build including Ruida's; `machine/cmd.py:17` (the module that owns `send_job`) imports `GcodeContext, JobInfo` at module level; `machine.py:33` imports `GcodeDialect` at runtime; and `ruida_driver.py:18` / `ruida_encoder.py:18` import `pipeline.encoder.base`, which executes `pipeline/encoder/__init__.py:3` -> `from .gcode import GcodeEncoder` -> `from ...machine.models.dialect import GcodeDialect`, so `gcode.py` and the whole dialect package are transitively loaded by every Ruida job. Ruida stays off the G-code branch only because `RuidaDriver.uses_gcode = False` (`ruida_driver.py:61`) makes `machine.dialect` return `None` (`profile.py:431` and `machine.py:1657-1658` set `dialect_uid = None`), so `intent_builder.py:815` falls through to `PythonEncoder(RuidaEncoder)`.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

The removal boundary is NOT the pipeline/encoder package — it is a single line inside it. `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/__init__.py:3` (`from .gcode import GcodeEncoder`) is the only thing that makes gcode.py and the dialect package reachable from the Ruida path, because `ruida_driver.py:18` and `ruida_encoder.py:18` import `pipeline.encoder.base`, and importing a submodule runs the package __init__. Deleting gcode.py without first editing that line breaks the Ruida driver at import time, which also breaks build_rd_bytes, send_job and the .rd export. Editing that one line touches no protected file.

MUST KEEP (reachable from the Ruida job path): pipeline/encoder/__init__.py (as a file), pipeline/encoder/base.py (ruida_encoder.py:197 calls MachineCodeOpMap.from_lists), pipeline/encoder/context.py (module-level import at the protected machine/cmd.py:17), pipeline/encoder/rust_helpers.py (module-level import at the protected intent_builder.py:79 — its functions are only *called* on the Grbl branch, but the import itself is in a protected file), the whole of machine/models/dialect/** and dialect_manager.py (machine.py:33 imports GcodeDialect at runtime, Machine.__init__ defaults dialect_uid='grbl' and connects to dialects_changed at machine.py:204-206, from_dict runs migrate_builtin_dialect_to_copy at :1670-1672, and profile.py:385 validates dialect templates), shared/gcodeedit/** (GcodeViewer/GcodeEditor/GcodeHighlighter render the Ruida job preview — mainwindow.py:1348-1349), shared/util/template.py (protected cmd.py:18), and ui_gtk/machine/console.py (imports nothing gcode; commands go to machine.run_raw, which RuidaDriver implements at :689).

REMOVABLE (only reachable from G-code drivers or the dialect UI): pipeline/encoder/gcode.py plus tests/pipeline/encoder/test_gcode_encoder.py; and the dialect-editing UI chain ui_gtk/machine/gcode_settings_page.py -> dialect_list.py -> dialect_editor.py -> template_selector.py, which is already hidden at runtime for Ruida by settings_dialog.py:300-317 and has no test importers.

SAFE REMOVAL ORDER for the gcode encoder: (1) edit pipeline/encoder/__init__.py to drop the `.gcode` import and the "GcodeEncoder" entry in __all__ — verify by importing rayforge.machine.driver.ruida.ruida_driver; (2) remove the seven `create_encoder` implementations that return GcodeEncoder (dummy.py:86-89, grbl_network.py:155-158, grbl_serial.py:190-193, grbl_serial_simple.py:177-179, marlin_serial.py:132-134, octoprint_driver.py:153-155, smoothie.py:111-114) together with their driver modules and their tests; (3) only then delete gcode.py. For the UI: (1) drop the `_gcode_row`/`_gcode_stack_page` block in settings_dialog.py (:26, :63-64, :167-169, :237-238, :272, :285, :300-317); (2) delete gcode_settings_page.py, dialect_list.py, dialect_editor.py, template_selector.py in that order.

THREE TRAPS. First: dummy.py / NoDeviceDriver is a G-code driver (dummy.py:86-89 returns GcodeEncoder), and intent_builder.py:978/980 falls back to it whenever the configured driver cannot be resolved — deleting GcodeEncoder without giving NoDeviceDriver a replacement encoder turns an unresolvable-driver case into an import error inside the protected job path. Second: settings_dialog.py:237-238 grabs the G-code sidebar row by hard-coded index 3 (`get_row_at_index(3)`); removing an earlier sidebar row silently re-points that reference at the wrong row. Third: the 'Hooks & Macros' page (settings_dialog.py:172-174) is NOT gated by uses_gcode even though it is titled "G-code Hooks" (hook_list.py:19) and opens GcodeEditorDialog — it stays visible and functional for Ruida because macros run through machine.run_raw, so gcode_editor.py / hook_list.py / macro_list.py / hooks_macros_page.py must NOT be swept up as "gcode UI". Also note pipeline/encoder/gcode.py and ui_gtk/machine/gcode_editor.py define unrelated things, and shared/gcodeedit/editor.py's GcodeEditor is a different class from gcode_editor.py's GcodeEditorDialog (confirmed: gcode_editor.py imports no gcodeedit module).

### External dependencies

- PyYAML (yaml) — rayforge/machine/models/dialect_manager.py:7. ALSO used elsewhere in the repo: rayforge/machine/device/profile.py (manifest loading) and machine/config persistence, so it is not exclusive to this subsystem.
- blinker (Signal) — dialect_manager.py:8 (dialects_changed), shared/gcodeedit/editor.py:3, shared/gcodeedit/viewer.py:6, ui_gtk/machine/console.py:5. ALSO used app-wide (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_client.py:13, ruida_transport.py:10, core models). Not exclusive.
- PyGObject / gi.repository (Gtk, Adw, Gdk, GLib, Pango) — every UI module in this area (dialect_editor.py:6, dialect_list.py:4, template_selector.py:4, gcode_editor.py:3, console.py:6, gcodeedit/editor.py:4, gcodeedit/highlighter.py:5, gcodeedit/viewer.py:7). ALSO the entire rayforge/ui_gtk tree. Not exclusive.
- numpy — rayforge/pipeline/encoder/base.py:5 (MachineCodeOpMap's int32 arrays). ALSO rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:48 and rayforge/machine/cmd.py:8. Not exclusive.
- raygeo (raygeo.ops.Ops, raygeo.ops.convert.GcodeDialectSpec, raygeo.geo.types.Point3D/Rect) — encoder/base.py:6, encoder/gcode.py:3, encoder/rust_helpers.py:13-14, encoder/context.py:5. raygeo is the core Rust geometry/pipeline engine used by the Ruida encoder too (ruida_encoder.py:13-16). Not exclusive. NOTE: raygeo.ops.convert.GcodeSpec / GcodeDialectSpec / Ops.to_gcode are the Rust-side G-code encoder; they live outside this repo and are imported by the protected intent_builder.py:63-68.
- No third-party package is used ONLY by the gcode subsystem — removing it frees no dependency.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (24)

- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:76 — `from ..machine.models.dialect import GRBL_DIALECT`. Module-level RUNTIME import (not TYPE_CHECKING) in the protected job path. Every Ruida job build executes it.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:79 — `from .encoder.rust_helpers import build_encode_context, dialect_to_spec`. Module-level runtime import in the protected job path.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:88 — TYPE_CHECKING import of GcodeDialect (annotation for _is_grbl).
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:78 — `from .encoder.base import EncodedOutput` (the Ruida-shared half of the encoder package).
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:805-821 — _build_encoder: reads `machine.dialect` (line 814) and branches on `_is_grbl(dialect)` (line 815) for EVERY machine, then falls through to `PythonEncoder(self._make_python_encoder_callable(machine, doc), 'driver.encode')` at :818-821 — this is the branch Ruida takes.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:823-844 — _grbl_encoder_spec: builds raygeo GcodeSpec via dialect_to_spec + build_encode_context. Grbl-only, never entered for Ruida.

- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:16-18 — module-level imports of pipeline.encoder.base.EncodedOutput, pipeline.encoder.context.GcodeContext/JobInfo, and shared.util.template.TemplateFormatter. This module owns send_job (cmd.py:369) and run_send_job (cmd.py:385).

This import EXECUTES rayforge/pipeline/encoder/__init__.py, whose line 3 imports gcode.py, whose line 5 imports the dialect package. This is the transitive gcode reachability from the Ruida driver.

- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:255-257 — `create_encoder` returns RuidaEncoder() and ignores machine.dialect entirely.

<details><summary>Module notes (42)</summary>

- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/__init__.py` - MUST KEEP AS A FILE. This is the exact import boundary: line 3 `from .gcode import GcodeEncoder` is what drags gcode.py and the dialect package into every Ruida import. Deleting gcode.py without editing this line breaks the Ruida driver at import time. Editing line 3 alone is safe and touches no protected file.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/base.py` - KEEP. Not a gcode module despite the package it lives in. base.py:40-59 MachineCodeOpMap.from_lists exists specifically for the Ruida encoder (its own docstring says so) and is called at ruida_encoder.py:197.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/gcode.py` - REMOVABLE only with the 7 G-code drivers. No Ruida file names it. Reached from Ruida ONLY through pipeline/encoder/__init__.py:3. NOTE: dummy.py (NoDeviceDriver) is a G-code driver here — it is the fallback used at intent_builder.py:978/980 when the configured driver cannot be resolved, so removing gcode.py forces a decision about NoDeviceDriver.create_encoder (dummy.py:86-89).
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/rust_helpers.py` - KEEP AS-IS. intent_builder.py:79 is a module-level import in the protected pipeline job path. Both symbols are only *called* on the Grbl branch (intent_builder.py:840-843 and gcode.py:23-24), but removing the file would require editing intent_builder.py:79, which is protected.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/context.py` - KEEP. rayforge/machine/cmd.py is the protected send_job module (send_job at cmd.py:369, run_send_job at :385); its line 17 is a module-level import used by execute_macro_by_uid at cmd.py:643-648. Removing context.py would require editing a protected file.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/__init__.py` - KEEP. Directly imported at module level by the protected intent_builder.py:76 and by machine.py:33.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/base.py` - KEEP. profile.py:385 calls GcodeDialect.validate_template_dict when loading any device manifest, including Ruida device dirs (it is skipped only when dialect.yaml is absent AND uses_gcode is False).
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/grbl.py` - KEEP. GRBL_DIALECT.uid is the value _is_grbl compares against at intent_builder.py:1105 on every job build.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/grbl_dynamic.py` - Reachable only via BUILTIN_DIALECTS (dialect/__init__.py:12-20) which is consumed by dialect_manager and template_selector.py:6.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/grbl_raster.py` - Same as grbl_dynamic.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/linuxcnc.py` - Same as grbl_dynamic.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/mach4_m67.py` - Same as grbl_dynamic, plus a direct test import.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/marlin.py` - Same as grbl_dynamic.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect/smoothieware.py` - Same as grbl_dynamic.
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect_manager.py` - KEEP. Every Machine (Ruida included) connects to dialects_changed in __init__ (machine.py:204-206) and disconnects at :505-506. Machine.from_dict runs migrate_builtin_dialect_to_copy unconditionally at machine.py:1670-1672.
- `rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/__init__.py` - No importer uses the package name — every consumer imports the submodule directly (bottom_panel.py:14 uses ...shared.gcodeedit.viewer). This __init__ re-export surface is unreferenced; that is a fact, not a recommendation to delete it.
- `rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/viewer.py` - KEEP — used by Ruida. mainwindow.py:1348-1349 feeds final_artifact.machine_code into it, and for a Ruida machine machine_code is RuidaEncoder's human-readable command text (EncodedOutput.text, artifact/job.py:81-86). The op_map it consumes is built by ruida_encoder.py:197.
- `rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/editor.py` - KEEP (transitively used by Ruida through GcodeViewer). NOTE: the dialog GcodeEditorDialog in ui_gtk/machine/gcode_editor.py is a DIFFERENT class and does NOT import this one — I confirmed gcode_editor.py's imports at lines 1-9.
- `rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/highlighter.py` - KEEP (transitively used by Ruida). It highlights whatever text is in the buffer, including Ruida command text.
- `rayforge/shared/util/template.py` - KEEP. Module-level import in the protected send_job module; used at cmd.py:648 by execute_macro_by_uid, which then calls machine.run_raw — and RuidaDriver implements run_raw (ruida_driver.py:689), so macros are cross-driver.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/gcode_settings_page.py` - REMOVABLE for Ruida. Already hidden at runtime for Ruida by settings_dialog.py:300-317 (_update_gcode_page_visibility keys off driver_cls.uses_gcode, which is False at ruida_driver.py:61).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/dialect_list.py` - REMOVABLE. This is the only place in the app that calls machine.set_dialect_uid (dialect_list.py:93-94). No test imports it.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/dialect_editor.py` - REMOVABLE. Only importer is dialect_list.py. No test imports it.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/template_selector.py` - REMOVABLE with dialect_editor.py + dialect_list.py. No test imports it.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/gcode_editor.py` - NOT SAFELY REMOVABLE despite the name. It is reached from the 'Hooks & Macros' settings page, which is NOT gated by uses_gcode (settings_dialog.py:172-174 adds it unconditionally and the sidebar row at :242-244 is never hidden), so a Ruida user can open it. It uses plain Gtk.TextView, not the shared GcodeEditor widget.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/hook_list.py` - Named G-code but driver-agnostic in fact: it edits Macro objects consumed by rust_helpers._build_macro_table and by cmd.execute_macro_by_uid -> machine.run_raw, which Ruida implements. Visible for Ruida.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/macro_list.py` - Same as hook_list.py — visible for Ruida.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/hooks_macros_page.py` - Not gated by uses_gcode — unlike the G-code page. Visible for Ruida.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/console.py` - KEEP — NOT a G-code module. It imports nothing from the gcode or dialect packages (confirmed console.py:1-18); commands go out via command_submitted -> bottom_panel.py:295-298 -> machine.run_raw, which RuidaDriver implements at ruida_driver.py:689. Sensitivity keys only off is_connected / NoDeviceDriver (console.py:563-571).
- `rayforge/resources/icons/gcode-symbolic.svg` - Still needed by bottom_panel.py:182 (the preview tab used for Ruida) even if the settings page goes away.
- `tests/pipeline/encoder/test_gcode_encoder.py` - Imports rayforge.pipeline.encoder.gcode:8 and rayforge.machine.models.dialect.grbl:7. Dies with gcode.py.
- `tests/pipeline/encoder/test_machine_code_op_map.py` - KEEP — covers the class the Ruida encoder builds via from_lists.
- `tests/machine/models/test_dialect.py` - KEEP with the dialect model.
- `tests/machine/models/test_dialect_manager.py` - KEEP with dialect_manager.py.
- `tests/machine/models/dialects/test_dialect_gcode.py` - Pure G-code output tests. Seven .gcode fixtures live in tests/machine/models/dialects/data/ (expected_square_grbl, _grbl_dynamic, _grbl_raster, _linuxcnc, _mach4_m67, _marlin, _smoothieware).
- `tests/shared/test_template.py` - KEEP — covers the macro expansion used by the protected cmd.execute_macro_by_uid.
- `tests/config/dialects/23dc0985-8851-49aa-812d-95692000b81e.yaml` - conftest monkeypatches config.DIALECT_DIR to a temp dir at those three lines; also patched in tests/machine/driver/marlin/test_marlin_serial_driver.py:161,:180, tests/machine/driver/octoprint/test_octoprint_driver.py:35, tests/machine/models/test_coordinate_spaces.py:47, tests/machine/models/test_machine_model.py:47.
- `tests/config/dialects/9d88d7d1-6853-4e81-a139-cfb4b6abb3cc.yaml` - Same as above.
- `website/docs/machine/gcode.md` - I did not trace the docusaurus sidebar wiring; treat inbound_refs as unverified rather than empty.
- `website/docs/reference/gcode-dialects.md` - Same caveat as above.
- `website/docs/general-info/gcode-basics.md` - Same caveat as above.
- `website/docs/machine/hooks-macros.md` - Describes a page that stays visible for Ruida.

</details>

<a id="multi-machine"></a>

## multi-machine

### Summary

Rayforge is multi-machine by construction: `MachineManager` (rayforge/machine/models/manager.py) owns a `dict[str, Machine]` persisted one-YAML-per-machine under `MACHINE_DIR` (~/.config/rayforge/machines/*.yaml, rayforge/config.py:21), and the "current machine" is a single pointer `Config.machine` (rayforge/core/config.py:57), persisted as a machine id in config.yaml and reachable app-wide as `get_context().config.machine` / `get_context().machine` (rayforge/context.py:89). Creation goes through the 11-step `UnifiedWizard` (rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/unified_wizard.py), which builds an in-memory `DeviceProfile` and commits via `DeviceProfile.create_machine()` (rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:398) — that call itself does `context.machine_mgr.add_machine(m)` at profile.py:403, so the machine is persisted at "Create Machine". Listing/deleting lives on the Application Settings → Machines page (rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/machine_settings_page.py); switching lives on the header-bar `MachineDropdown` → `MachineManager.set_active_machine()` (manager.py:126), which disconnects the old machine, sets `config.set_machine(new)` on the main thread, then auto-connects the new one. Per-machine editing is `MachineSettingsDialog` (rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py), a 12-page sidebar dialog. `DeviceProfile`s are read-only templates shipped in rayforge/resources/devices/** (28 dirs, each device.yaml [+ optional dialect.yaml]) plus user-installed ones in ~/.config/rayforge/devices, discovered by `DeviceProfileManager` (rayforge/machine/device/manager.py). "Discovery" of actual hardware is only two things: serial-port enumeration (`SerialTransport.list_ports()` surfaced through the `SerialPortVar` combo adapter) and driver-side `probe()` — there is NO network scan / mDNS / zeroconf anywhere in the repo.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

DELETING THE MULTI-MACHINE UI IS CHEAP; DELETING THE MULTI-MACHINE MODEL IS NOT.

What breaks if you delete the UI layer (unified_wizard.py + wizard_pages/** + machine_dropdown.py + settings/machine_settings_page.py + profile_importer.py + lbdev_import_dialog.py): four call sites, all confirmed. (1) rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/settings_dialog.py:14,83 imports and registers MachineSettingsPage. (2) rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:54,216,217,1046 constructs MachineDropdown, plus mainwindow.py:724,747,755,769 call machine_selector.update_eta() — the running-job ETA display dies with the dropdown unless that call is rehomed. (3) tests/ui_gtk/machine/test_unified_wizard.py (704 lines) fails to import. (4) rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py:334 (Export Machine Profile) and wizard_pages/profile_page.py:95 are the only device_profile_mgr consumers in the UI; drop them and DeviceProfileManager becomes reachable only from context.py. Nothing in the pipeline / job path imports any of these modules — verified by grep across rayforge/, tests/, scripts/, data/, debian/, snap/, website/.

What a single-fixed-machine app MUST KEEP, because the whole app reads through it: (a) `Config.machine` (rayforge/core/config.py:57) and `context.machine` (context.py:89) — 81 `config.machine` references across 14 modules including doceditor/editor.py, doceditor/file_cmd.py, doceditor/stock_cmd.py, machine/cmd.py, camera/manager.py, debug.py and two sketcher addon modules. (b) `MachineManager.get_controller()` (manager.py:57), because Machine.controller (machine.py:225) and Machine.has_controller (machine.py:235) delegate to it — every jog / Start-Pause-Stop / send path goes through a MachineController that only MachineManager can mint. (c) `machine_mgr.machines` as a container, because rayforge/machine/driver/driver.py:464 walks all other machines on every connect() to raise ResourceBusyError on a shared serial/network resource — with one machine this loop is a no-op but the attribute must exist. (d) save/load of the machine YAML (manager.py:213/225/253) — that is where every machine setting persists. (e) DeviceProfile.create_machine (profile.py:398) if you want built-in device templates at all; note it calls machine_mgr.add_machine at L403.

SAFE REMOVAL ORDER (if the goal is a one-machine app): 1) mainwindow.py — rehome the four update_eta() calls, then drop the MachineDropdown import/construction/connection (54, 216-217, 1046-1047) and the on_machine_selected_by_selector method. 2) settings/settings_dialog.py:14,83 — unregister MachineSettingsPage. 3) delete machine_dropdown.py and settings/machine_settings_page.py. 4) delete unified_wizard.py + wizard_pages/** + tests/ui_gtk/machine/test_unified_wizard.py together (they are a closed graph; profile_importer.py/lbdev_import_dialog.py have only profile_page.py as a caller and can go with them, along with the already-dead open_profile_zip alias). 5) ONLY THEN consider MachineManager: keep the class, collapse it to a single machine, and leave set_active_machine as a no-op rather than removing it — core/config.py:355 subscribes to machine_removed at construction time and will KeyError/AttributeError if the signal disappears. 6) Never touch rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py, the three Ruida device.yaml files, or anything under machine/driver/ruida/.

TRAPS FOUND WHILE MAPPING (report only, not fixed): mainwindow.py:1146 passes initial_page=\"hours\" to MachineSettingsDialog, but no sidebar page is named \"hours\" (the counters page is \"maintenance\"), so that action silently opens the first page. unified_wizard.py:8 documents a `machine_created` signal that does not exist (it is `profile_created`, L81/L380). rayforge/machine/device/__init__.py re-exports four names that nothing imports. profile_importer.open_profile_zip (L60) has zero callers. context.py:242's active-machine tiebreak is min-by-uuid, so on a fresh multi-machine config the 'first' machine is arbitrary.

### External dependencies

- blinker==1.9.0 (requirements.txt:4) — Signal used by MachineManager (machine_added/removed/updated), MachineDropdown.machine_selected, UnifiedWizard.profile_created, and every WizardPage's ready_changed. Used repo-wide (131 files import yaml/blinker); NOT exclusive to this area.
- PyGObject==3.56.3 (requirements.txt:12) — Gtk/Adw/Gio/Gdk/GLib/Pango for every UI module here. Used by the whole ui_gtk tree; not exclusive.
- PyYAML==6.0.3 (requirements.txt:19) — machine YAML persistence (machine/models/manager.py), device.yaml/dialect.yaml parsing (machine/device/profile.py, manager.py), lbdev install. Also used by rayforge/debug.py, core/config.py, addon manifests; not exclusive.
- pyserial==3.5 (requirements.txt:17) — reaches this area only indirectly, via serial.tools.list_ports in rayforge/machine/transport/serial.py:10, which powers the serial-port combo the wizard's ConnectionPage renders. Owned by the transport layer, also used by every serial driver; not exclusive.
- stdlib only for the rest: zipfile/shutil/tempfile (device profile install/export), uuid (Machine.id, provider_page), pathlib, dataclasses, inspect (driver registry).
- NO network-discovery dependency exists (no zeroconf/mdns/bonjour package in requirements.txt and no such code in rayforge/).

### PROTECTED touchpoints (12)

- rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:20 — `from .ruida import RuidaDriver`; this populates the module-level `drivers` list that BOTH driver selectors read (wizard controller_page.py:17 and settings general_preferences_page.py:6). Any change to the registry changes what the Ruida tile looks like. Read-only for me.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/controller_page.py:28 — `"RuidaDriver": "network-wired-symbolic"` in _DRIVER_ICONS. A string key only; no ruida module is imported by the wizard.
- rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:14 — `from ...machine.driver import get_driver_cls`; used at profile.py:376 (from_path, to decide whether dialect.yaml is required via driver_cls.uses_gcode) and profile.py:400-407 (create_machine -> m.set_driver(driver_cls, cfg.driver_args)). This is how a Ruida device profile ends up with a live RuidaDriver.
- rayforge/resources/devices/monport-60w-co2/device.yaml:6 — `driver: RuidaDriver` (also driver_args host/main_port 50200/jog_port 50210).
- rayforge/resources/devices/omtech-polar/device.yaml:6 — `driver: RuidaDriver`.
- rayforge/resources/devices/thunder-laser-nova35/device.yaml:6 — `driver: RuidaDriver`; this profile ships NO dialect.yaml (correct, since RuidaDriver.uses_gcode is False and profile.py only requires a dialect for G-code drivers).
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/unified_wizard.py:455-462 — the wizard consults `driver_cls.supports_probing` and specifically treats NoDeviceDriver as non-probeable; RuidaDriver does not set supports_probing, so the wizard skips the probe step for Ruida machines. Behavior depends on the protected driver's class attributes.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py:288-309 — _update_maturity_banner and _update_gcode_page_visibility read driver_cls.maturity and driver_cls.uses_gcode; for RuidaDriver this is what hides the G-code page. Read-only dependency on the protected driver.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/head_page.py — seeds MachineConfig.heads including max power, which feeds the protected Min/Max power semantics once the machine is live. The wizard only writes profile data; it does not touch the jog panel or power UI.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/machine_dropdown.py:113-124 — the picker lives in the same header bar as, and its status text/ETA is fed from, the job lifecycle (mainwindow.py:724/747/755/769 update_eta). It reads DeviceStatus/TransportStatus from the driver layer but does not participate in build_rd_bytes/send_job.
- rayforge/machine/models/manager.py:126-160 — set_active_machine disconnects the previously active machine before switching; this is the code path that would interact with an in-flight Ruida job/connection. It is machine-management code, but its effect lands on the protected send path.
- rayforge/machine/models/manager.py:57-80 / machine.py:225 — get_controller is the lazy factory for MachineController, which is what the jog panel and Start/Pause/Stop ultimately drive.

- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/unified_wizard.py` - Only ONE production entry point: the Add Machine button on the Machines settings page. Module docstring (L8) says it emits `machine_created`; the real signal is `profile_created` (L81/L380) — stale doc. do_close_request returns False (L?) so the dialog cannot be closed by the WM close button path in the usual way; close() is explicit.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/__init__.py` - Every wizard page imports WizardPage/_makePreferencesGroup from here; also imported by the other pages not listed (hardware/head/rotary/camera/ai_lookup) via `from . import WizardPage, _makePreferencesGroup`.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/profile_page.py` - This is the machine-template picker. next_on_footer=False, so the generic Next button is hidden here.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/controller_page.py` - _DRIVER_ICONS (L25-34) hard-codes an icon per driver classname, including 'RuidaDriver' at L28 — a PROTECTED-adjacent string reference (name only, no ruida code imported).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/connection_page.py` - This page is where serial-port enumeration surfaces for serial drivers, via the SerialPortVar -> SerialPortAdapter combo (rayforge/ui_gtk/varset/adapter/combo.py:123).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/probe_page.py` - This is half of the app's real device DISCOVERY. Auto-starts the probe once on first entry (L101-103).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/ai_lookup_page.py` - 462 lines; largest wizard page.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/hardware_page.py` - Name-collides with the unrelated rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/hardware_page.py used by MachineSettingsDialog. Axis reversal goes into wizard.aux_state['reverse'] and is applied post-creation in _materialize_machine (unified_wizard.py:388-395).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/head_page.py` - Touches Min/Max power semantics (PROTECTED area) at the profile level only.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/camera_page.py` - This is a third, camera-specific device enumeration (V4L), separate from serial/probe discovery.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/machine_dropdown.py` - No test file references it. Selection is fed to MachineManager.set_active_machine via mainwindow.on_machine_selected_by_selector (mainwindow.py:1076-1082). update_eta is called from mainwindow.py:724, 747, 755, 769 — so removing this widget breaks four ETA call sites.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py` - BUG worth flagging (not mine to fix): mainwindow.py:1146 passes initial_page="hours", but no sidebar page is named "hours" (the counters page is "maintenance"), so the Machine Hours notification action silently lands on the first page. No test file targets this dialog.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/machine_settings_page.py` - _on_profile_selected_for_add (L205) receives the wizard's profile_created signal and opens MachineSettingsDialog on the freshly created machine; it has a `if machine is None: profile.create_machine(...)` fallback that is dead in practice because the wizard always supplies the machine.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/profile_importer.py` - open_profile_zip (L60) is an explicitly-labelled legacy alias with ZERO callers anywhere in rayforge/ or tests/ — dead code (reporting only, not deleting).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/lbdev_import_dialog.py` - Single caller. No test.
- `rayforge/machine/device/profile.py` - create_machine() has a side effect the name does not advertise: it persists the machine into MachineManager immediately (L403). Drivers are also returned by grbl/marlin probe() as DeviceProfile instances, so this type is on the probe path too.
- `rayforge/machine/device/manager.py` - Instantiated exactly once, lazily, in context.device_profile_mgr with [BUILTIN_DEVICES_DIR, USER_DEVICES_DIR] and install_dir=USER_DEVICES_DIR.
- `rayforge/machine/device/__init__.py` - VERIFIED: nothing imports `rayforge.machine.device` as a package — every consumer imports the submodules (machine.device.profile / .manager / .lightburn_importer) directly. The re-export surface is unused.
- `rayforge/machine/models/manager.py` - This is the single most load-bearing multi-machine object. _safe_connect (L100) explicitly distinguishes 'active machine' from 'inactive machine' when a serial resource is busy.
- `rayforge/core/config.py` - PARTIALLY IN MY AREA — this file is the answer to 'where is the current machine held', but it is shared config infrastructure, not multi-machine-only code.
- `rayforge/context.py` - The 'first machine wins' tiebreak is min-by-uuid, not by name or creation order — nondeterministic from the user's point of view.
- `rayforge/resources/devices` - 3 of the 28 are RuidaDriver profiles (monport-60w-co2, omtech-polar, thunder-laser-nova35), each with driver_args host/main_port 50200/jog_port 50210. thunder-laser-nova35 has NO dialect.yaml (correct — Ruida is not a G-code driver).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/varset/adapter/combo.py` - PARTIALLY IN MY AREA — it is the discovery surface, but it is shared varset infrastructure used by both the wizard's ConnectionPage and the settings dialog's GeneralPreferencesPage.
- `rayforge/machine/transport/serial.py` - PARTIALLY IN MY AREA — this is the only host-device enumeration in the app. It is transport code, shared with all serial drivers.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py` - PROTECTED TOUCHPOINT: L20 imports RuidaDriver from .ruida, so `drivers` (and therefore the controller tile grid and the settings-dialog driver combo) includes Ruida. Do not touch this file.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/general_preferences_page.py` - Uses drivers.index(driver_cls) (L121) to map the class to a combo index — position-sensitive coupling to the registry's list order.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/template_selector.py` - Included only to rule it out of this area: it belongs to the G-code dialect subsystem, not to multi-machine management.
- `tests/ui_gtk/machine/test_unified_wizard.py` - The only test coverage for any wizard/selector UI in this area.
- `tests/machine/models/test_machine_profile.py` - Any change to the shipped device profiles is caught here.
- `website/docs/application-settings/machines.md` - Describes exactly the machine_settings_page.py behavior confirmed above.

</details>
