<a id="extras"></a>

## extras — (a) rotary, (b) probe/autofocus, (c) addon manager + builtin addons, (d) AI workpiece / ai_lookup / core.ai

### Summary

Four independent "extras" candidates, and only one of them is actually cheap to remove. (a) ROTARY is NOT a leaf: `RotaryModule` is a first-class field on `Machine`, `Layer` carries `rotary_enabled`/`rotary_diameter`/`rotary_module_uid`, `MachineCapability.ROTARY` is derived from it, and the PROTECTED pipeline job path (`rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:77` imports `RotaryMode`/`RotaryType`; `:911` and `:1140` call `machine.get_rotary_module_for_layer`) plus `machine/kinematics.py`, `machine/kinematic_mapping.py`, `machine/assembly.py`, the whole sim3d renderer stack and `doceditor/editor.py` reconciliation all read it. Removing it means editing protected files. (b) There are TWO unrelated things called "probe": device-discovery probing (`grbl_probe.py`, `marlin_probe.py`, `probe_page.py` — reads `$I`/`$$`/`M115` to build a `DeviceProfile`, wired into the machine wizard) and a Z-touch-off cycle (`Driver.run_probe_cycle`) which every driver implements but which has ZERO UI caller — only tests reach it. The Ruida driver has no probe or autofocus at all: `ruida_driver.py:1577` is a stub that emits "Probe not supported". (c) The addon system is load-bearing, not optional: `laser_essentials` supplies `ContourStep`/`EngraveStep`/etc. that `rayforge/doceditor/step_cmd.py:168,193,194`, `rayforge/core/recipe.py:26-29` and `rayforge/image/assembler.py:218,220` look up BY NAME, and `post_processors` supplies transformers that `ContourStep.get_default_transformers_dicts` hard-`assert`s non-None. Only `cnc_essentials` and `tool_library` are marked experimental/disabled-by-default. (d) The AI subsystem is genuinely peripheral: `rayforge/core/ai/**` is reached only by three UI surfaces (wizard AI-lookup/provider pages, the AI settings page) and the `ai_workpiece_generator` addon, and nothing in the job path, layer/step system, or Ruida driver touches it.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Ranked from safest to most dangerous.

SAFEST — (b) Z-PROBE CYCLE (not the wizard probing). `Driver.run_probe_cycle` + `probe_status_changed` (rayforge/machine/driver/driver.py:274, :762) and its seven driver implementations have NO production caller anywhere in rayforge/. Only tests reach them. There is no jog-panel, menu, or toolbar Z-probe/autofocus entry point, and the Ruida driver's implementation is a "not supported" stub. Deleting the cycle would break only tests. Safe removal order: (1) delete the per-driver overrides (dummy.py:243, grbl_network.py:809, grbl_serial.py:1318, grbl_serial_simple.py:747, marlin_serial.py:609, octoprint_driver.py:822, smoothie.py:480) EXCEPT the protected ruida_driver.py:1577 which must stay; (2) delete the base method and signal in driver.py. Since ruida_driver.py is protected and overrides it, in practice you must LEAVE the base declaration in place — so this yields little. Recommend: report as dead code, do not remove.

SAFE — (c) the two EXPERIMENTAL addons. `rayforge-addon-cnc` (cnc_essentials, 43 .py) and `rayforge-addon-tools` (tool_library, 10 .py) both carry `default_state: disabled` + `maturity: experimental` and have ZERO inbound references from rayforge/ core. tool_library's consumers use `service_registry.get("tool_manager")` with a None guard. cnc_essentials declares `requires: post_processors`, so removing it removes a dependent but nothing depends on it. Removal order: delete the addon directory, then delete its tests under the addon dir. Nothing else changes.

SAFE-ISH — (d) AI. `rayforge/core/ai/**` (5 modules) + `ai_lookup_page.py` + `provider_page.py` + `ai_settings_page.py` + `rayforge-addon-ai-workpiece/` form a closed set: nothing in the pipeline, layer/step system, machine model, or any driver imports them. Removal order: (1) delete the `rayforge-addon-ai-workpiece/` directory and scripts/screenshot/ai_workpiece_generator.py + its cli.py:18 entry; (2) remove the wizard steps — delete ai_lookup_page.py and provider_page.py, then edit rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/unified_wizard.py to drop imports :26/:38/:46, the "ai_lookup"/"ai_provider" entries in the step list (:61 area), the dispatch at :219-221, and the is_ai_configured() routing at :418-424 and :471-473; (3) delete ai_settings_page.py and its two lines in settings/settings_dialog.py (:10 import, :87 _add_page); (4) delete rayforge/core/ai/ and the context properties at rayforge/context.py:11-12, :60-61, :197-217, :406-407; (5) delete tests/core/ai/. Breakage if done wrong: tests/ui_gtk/machine/test_unified_wizard.py asserts the exact wizard step sequence in eight places (:69-70, :107, :135, :170, :183, :205-206, :253) — every one of those needs updating. aiohttp stays (five other users). Note the sketcher addon loses its only dependent, so it becomes disable-able.

DO NOT REMOVE — (b) DEVICE-DISCOVERY probing. `grbl_probe.py` is the shared probe orchestration for ALL THREE GRBL drivers (grbl_network.py:96, grbl_serial.py:199, grbl_serial_simple.py:185) and `marlin_probe.py` for Marlin (marlin_serial.py:143). `probe_page.py` is wizard Step 4. Deleting any of it removes firmware auto-detection for every GRBL and Marlin machine. It is not "autofocus" and should not be treated as an extra.

DO NOT REMOVE — (c) laser_essentials and post_processors. Core resolves ContourStep/EngraveStep BY NAME in rayforge/doceditor/step_cmd.py, rayforge/core/recipe.py and rayforge/image/assembler.py; ContourStep hard-asserts seven post_processors transformers. The Ruida job path itself calls step.build_compute_payload() and step.assembler_token_params() (intent_builder.py:498, :528) — both implemented only in the laser addon. Deleting either addon means no steps can be created and no job can be built. Note a latent bug worth reporting rather than fixing: AddonManager.can_disable (addon_manager.py:1558) only checks addon→addon `requires`, so the Settings > Addons toggle will happily let a user disable laser_essentials, after which step_cmd.py:168 silently returns and new layers get no default step.

MOST DANGEROUS — (a) ROTARY. It is not a subsystem you can lift out; it is a field on Machine, three fields on Layer, an enum member of MachineCapability, a branch in the kinematics/assembly builder, a branch in the PROTECTED intent_builder, and a structural `is_rotary` axis threaded through every sim3d renderer. Deleting RotaryModule requires edits inside rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py (protected) and rayforge/core/layer.py (protected layer/step system), plus rewriting the sim3d renderer stack which currently pairs every ops/ring/overlay/texture renderer by rotary flag. The only genuinely removable rotary leaves are: rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/rotary_module_page.py (27.6 KB, reached only from machine settings_dialog.py:33/:187), rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/rotary_page.py (reached only from unified_wizard.py:48/:227), rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/cylinder_renderer.py (reached only from scene_renderer.py:41), and the two screenshot scripts. Removing the UI pages leaves the model, serialisation, kinematics and job-path branches intact but makes rotary unconfigurable — a half-removal that will read as a bug. Recommend treating rotary as OUT OF SCOPE for deletion, or scoping the work to hiding the two UI pages only.

Also worth noting: rayforge/machine/kinematic_mapping.py (11.6 KB) has no inbound reference from any non-test file that I could find by grepping its module name or its symbols (KinematicMapping, from_rotary_module). Its only confirmed importer is tests/machine/test_kinematic_mapping.py. I could NOT prove it is dead — a caller may reach it through a re-export I did not trace — so verify before acting on it.

### External dependencies

- numpy — (a) rotary_module.py uses it for axis_position/transform matrices. Used repo-wide (sim3d, pipeline, machine); removing rotary frees nothing.
- blinker (Signal) — (a) rotary_module.py, (b) grbl_probe.py/marlin_probe.py/probe_page.py, (c) addon_manager.py/update_cmd.py, (d) ai_service.py. Used repo-wide; not exclusive to any of these areas.
- raygeo (raygeo.geo.types.Rect3D, raygeo.ops.axis.Axis) — (a) rotary_module.py. Core geometry package used across machine/ and pipeline/; not rotary-exclusive.
- pyyaml — (c) addon.py, addon_manager.py, core/addon_config.py; (d) core/ai/config.py. Also used by machine profiles and device manifests. Not exclusive.
- pluggy — (c) addon_manager.py is one of six users. Others: rayforge/context.py, rayforge/core/hooks.py, rayforge/core/registration.py, rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/post_processor/registry.py, rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-laser/tests/conftest.py. The whole hook/registry architecture depends on it, so removing addon_mgr would NOT let you drop pluggy.
- semver — (c) addon_mgr/addon.py. Only one other user: rayforge/shared/util/versioning.py. Closest thing to an addon-specific dependency, but versioning.py would keep it.
- aiohttp — (d) core/ai/openai_provider.py. Also used by rayforge/machine/driver/grbl/grbl_network.py, rayforge/machine/driver/octoprint/octoprint_driver.py, rayforge/machine/transport/http.py, rayforge/ui_gtk/about.py, rayforge/updater.py. Removing all AI code would NOT let you drop aiohttp.
- PyGObject / gi (Gtk, Adw, Gio) — all UI modules in every area. Repo-wide.
- No area in this survey introduces a dependency that is used ONLY by that area.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (15)

- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:77 — `from ..machine.models.rotary_module import RotaryMode, RotaryType`. The PROTECTED job path imports the rotary model directly.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:911 — `module = machine.get_rotary_module_for_layer(layer)` inside the RotaryMappingSpec builder (per-layer rotary mapping, gear ratio at :912-916, axis/replaced-axis decision at :936-940).
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:1140 — `module = machine.get_rotary_module_for_layer(layer)` feeding the cache-invalidation payload key `rotary:{layer.uid}` (:1142-1151).
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:498 — `part, payload = step.build_compute_payload(self._machine, wp)`. The concrete implementation lives in the LASER ADDON at rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-laser/laser_essentials/steps/contour_step.py:119.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:528 — `return step.assembler_token_params(self._machine, wp)`. Implementation at rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-laser/laser_essentials/steps/contour_step.py:152.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:563 — `cls = transformer_registry.get(name)`; every name resolvable there comes from the POST_PROCESSORS addon worker.
- rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py:391-392 — `self._doc.has_rotary_layer and MachineCapability.ROTARY in self._machine.get_capabilities()` gates a pipeline branch.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:1577-1581 — run_probe_cycle stub returning None after sending 'Probe not supported'. Confirms the Ruida driver owns no probe/autofocus. Read-only observation; do not touch.
- tests/machine/driver/ruida/test_ruida_driver.py:734-745 — test_run_probe_cycle_not_supported asserts that stub. Protected test.
- rayforge/core/layer.py:52-54, :77-79, :96-98, :109-111, :253-270, :279-294, :308-311 — the PROTECTED layer/step system stores and serialises rotary_enabled / rotary_diameter / rotary_module_uid and converts surface mm to degrees.
- rayforge/doceditor/step_cmd.py:168, :193, :194 — `step_registry.get("ContourStep")` / `get("EngraveStep")`. The layer/step system resolves LASER ADDON classes by string name.
- rayforge/core/recipe.py:26-29 — recipe categories hard-code ["ContourStep", "FrameStep", "WavefrontStep", "ShrinkWrapStep", "EngraveStep", "MaterialTestStep"], all owned by the laser addon.
- rayforge/image/assembler.py:218, :220 — import-time step selection hard-codes "EngraveStep" / "ContourStep".
- rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-laser/laser_essentials/steps/contour_step.py:209-226 — get_default_transformers_dicts() `assert`s that Smooth, LeadInOutTransformer, TabOpsTransformer, CropTransformer, MergeLinesTransformer, Optimize and MultiPassTransformer are all registered. These come from rayforge-addon-post/post_processors/worker.py:24-36.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:285-286 — MachineCapability.ROTARY is added to get_capabilities() whenever rotary_modules is non-empty; :397-462 build_assembly_for_rotary / _layer_configured feed the kinematics used by the job path.

<a id="ui-panels"></a>

## ui-panels

### Summary

`rayforge/ui_gtk/` is the entire GTK4/libadwaita front end: 290 Python modules, no non-Python files. It is rooted in `mainwindow.py:136 MainWindow` (an `Adw.ApplicationWindow` created only from `rayforge/app.py:223` and `:278`), which assembles a `Gtk.PopoverMenuBar` from `main_menu.py:9 MainMenu`, a `MainToolbar` (`toolbar.py:18`), a `Gtk.Paned` whose start child is a `Gtk.Stack` holding the 2D `WorkSurface` / 3D `Canvas3D` view stack plus a floating right-panel overlay (WorkflowView + DocItemPropertiesWidget), and whose end child is the dockable `BottomPanel` (`doceditor/bottom_panel.py:54`) with six dock tabs (Layers, Assets, G-code Viewer, Console, Controls, Laser). Every menu item and toolbar button is a `win.*` `Gio.Action` registered in `actions.py:230 ActionManager.register_actions`, with keyboard accelerators in the `SHORTCUTS` dict at `actions.py:31`. Two multi-page sidebar windows sit above that: the app `SettingsWindow` (`settings/settings_dialog.py:20`, 8 built-in pages + live addon pages) and `MachineSettingsDialog` (`machine/settings_dialog.py:48`, 12 pages), plus two wizards — `machine/unified_wizard.py:70 UnifiedWizard` (11 pages in `machine/wizard_pages/`) and `camera/wizard/wizard.py:33 CameraWizard` (6 pages in `camera/wizard/`). Four registries in this tree are the addon extension surface and are wired by name from `rayforge/core/registration.py:95,103,111,119,130,138,146`; `shared/` (60 files), `canvas/`+`canvas2d/` (34), `sim3d/` (44) and `varset/` (17) are shared substrates that builtin addons import directly by absolute path.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Deleting rayforge/ui_gtk/ deletes the application: rayforge/app.py:223 and :278 construct MainWindow directly, and rayforge/core/registration.py names seven modules in this tree by dotted string (lines 95, 103, 111, 119, 130, 138, 146) as the addon registries, so the addon manager would fail to load any registry. rayforge/context.py:83 also imports ui_gtk/shared/theme_service.py, and rayforge/debug.py:39 imports ui_gtk/about.py's get_dependency_info. Beyond that, all five builtin addons (rayforge-addon-sketcher, -laser, -cnc, -post, -print-and-cut, -ai-workpiece, -tools) import ui_gtk by absolute path - shared/pref_rows (66 sites), doceditor/step_settings/rows and pages, doceditor/post_processor/groups, ui_gtk/canvas, ui_gtk/icons, ui_gtk/layout, shared/patched_dialog_window - so removing shared/ or the step_settings/post_processor packages breaks the addons before it breaks the core. Nothing outside Python references this tree: verified no packaging, .desktop, debian/, snap/ or data/ file mentions ui_gtk (the only repo-wide match outside rayforge/tests/scripts is `pixi.toml:151` excluding tests/ui_gtk from pytest).

Safe removal order, smallest blast radius first - only these five are genuinely orphaned, each verified by grepping both the module name and every class name across rayforge/, tests/, scripts/ and docs/:
1. rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/connection_status_widget.py (ConnectionStatusIconWidget, ConnectionStatusWidget) - zero refs.
2. rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/status_widget.py (MachineStatusIconWidget, MachineStatusWidget) - zero refs.
3. rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/icon_tab_widget.py (IconTabWidget) - zero refs; superseded by shared/dock_layout.py.
4. rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/round_button.py (RoundButton) - zero refs.
5. rayforge/ui_gtk/task_bar.py (TaskBar) - zero refs; it is the only importer of shared/progress_bar.py besides mainwindow.py:60, so remove it before touching progress_bar (progress_bar itself is live via mainwindow.py:507 and must stay).
Everything else in this area has at least one verified inbound reference. Two near-orphans worth flagging rather than deleting: rayforge/ui_gtk/varset/varset_editor.py and shared/piemenu.py, shared/expression_entry.py, shared/status_bar.py are reachable only from the sketcher addon, and shared/direction_preview.py / shared/histogram_preview.py only from the laser addon - deleting them silently breaks an addon, not the core app. Also note the re-exports at rayforge/ui_gtk/__init__.py:1-2 and rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/__init__.py:1-2 have no verified importer, but removing the lines would be a behaviour-neutral cleanup rather than a file deletion.

### External dependencies

- PyGObject (gi.repository: Gtk 4, Adw/libadwaita, Gdk, Gio, GLib, GObject, GdkPixbuf, Pango) - imported by essentially every file in ui_gtk; also used across rayforge/builtin_addons/*/ui_gtk/ and rayforge/shared/gcodeedit/. Not removable.
- pycairo (`import cairo`) - canvas/ and canvas2d/ drawing; also used by rayforge/image/* renderers outside this area.
- PyOpenGL (`OpenGL.GL`) - sim3d/ only, loaded lazily via sim3d/__init__.py:21 initialize(); the app degrades to _PlaceholderCanvas3D when absent. Also reached from rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/model_preview_widget.py.
- numpy - sim3d/ renderers and camera/capture_surface.py; used pervasively elsewhere in rayforge (pipeline, image).
- trimesh - only rayforge/ui_gtk/sim3d/renderer/model_renderer.py (.glb mesh loading). Verified no other importer in the repo.
- blinker (Signal) - signals across ui_gtk (bottom_panel, layers_tab, settings/registry, pref_rows/base ...); used throughout rayforge core too.
- raygeo (raygeo.ops.axis.Axis) - mainwindow.py:10 and doceditor/bottom_panel.py:8; a core rayforge dependency, not ui-only.

<a id="canvas"></a>

## canvas (rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/** and rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/**)

### Summary

The 2D canvas is a three-layer inheritance chain: `Canvas` (Gtk.DrawingArea, rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/canvas.py:52) owns a single `view_transform: Matrix` and all selection/transform interaction; `WorldSurface` (rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/worldsurface.py:12) adds the mm world, the grid/axis renderer, and the ONLY zoom/pan state (`zoom_level`, `pan_x_mm`, `pan_y_mm`) plus scroll/middle-drag/space-drag navigation; `WorkSurface` (rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:47) adds document elements, ops-render suppression during navigation, and app wiring. There is no camera object — the "camera" is three scalars on WorldSurface recomposed into a 3x3 matrix by `_rebuild_view_transform` (worldsurface.py:257-323) as `m_offset @ m_zoom @ m_scale @ pan_transform`, and every screen<->world conversion goes through that one matrix via `Canvas._get_world_coords` (canvas.py:171) or `view_transform.transform_point`. Zoom is expressed relative to a "fit-to-view" base scale computed by `AxisRenderer.get_content_layout` (canvas/axis.py:68), so the aspect-fit layout, not the camera, defines what zoom=1.0 means. There is zero animation, tick-callback or frame-clock usage anywhere in the 2D canvas (the repo's only `add_tick_callback` is a one-shot grab-focus in a sketcher addon), and there are NO tests at all for pan, zoom, snapping or rotation.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

ANSWERS TO THE SIX QUESTIONS.

(1) WHERE THE VIEW TRANSFORM LIVES. There is no camera class. Two levels:
- `Canvas.view_transform: Matrix` — declared at rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/canvas.py:76, initialized to `Matrix.identity()`. The base Canvas never changes it; it only reads it (do_snapshot L224, overlays L308/L333/L353, cursor angles L627/L630/L635, transform.py calls L1002/L1035).
- The actual camera state is three floats on WorldSurface: `zoom_level` (worldsurface.py:42), `pan_x_mm` (L43), `pan_y_mm` (L44), plus two derived cache fields `_last_view_scale_x`/`_last_view_scale_y` (L45-46) and the clamps `MIN_ZOOM_FACTOR = 0.1` (L22) / `MAX_PIXELS_PER_MM = 100.0` (L25). World extents `width_mm`/`height_mm` (L47-48) participate in the base scale.
METHODS THAT CHANGE ZOOM OR PAN (complete list):
- `WorldSurface.set_pan(pan_x_mm, pan_y_mm)` — worldsurface.py:141 (sets both pan fields, rebuilds, queue_draw)
- `WorldSurface.set_zoom(zoom_level)` — worldsurface.py:148 (unclamped; caller must clamp)
- `WorldSurface.reset_view()` — worldsurface.py:325 (set_pan(0,0) + set_zoom(1.0))
- `WorldSurface.on_scroll(...)` — worldsurface.py:201-237 (the only zoom gesture; clamps ppm, then zoom-to-cursor by calling set_zoom then set_pan)
- `WorldSurface.on_pan_update(...)` — worldsurface.py:455-491 (middle-drag pan)
- `WorldSurface.on_mouse_drag(...)` — worldsurface.py:407-439 (space+left-drag pan; duplicates the pan math of on_pan_update)
- `WorldSurface.set_size(w_mm,h_mm)` — worldsurface.py:157 (changes world extents, hence effective scale)
- `WorldSurface.do_size_allocate(...)` — worldsurface.py:239-255 (widget resize changes base scale)
- `WorldSurface._rebuild_view_transform()` — worldsurface.py:257-323 (the ONLY writer of `self.view_transform`, at L307)
- `WorkSurface.reset_view()` — surface.py:1201 (refits to machine bed, then super())
- `WorkSurface._center_on_rotary_axis()` — surface.py:1368-1372 (calls set_pan)
- `WorkSurface._rebuild_view_transform()` — surface.py:804 (override; delegates composition to super, then propagates scale changes)
- Direct assignment outside the class: `tests/canvasapp.py:359/419/420/439/442` assigns `canvas.view_transform` directly (demo app only).
The composition itself is worldsurface.py:281-304: `final_transform = m_offset @ m_zoom @ m_scale @ pan_transform` where m_offset = translation(content_x, content_y), m_zoom = scale(zoom, zoom), m_scale = translation(0, content_h) @ scale(scale_x, -scale_y) (the mandatory Y-flip), pan_transform = translation(-pan_x, -pan_y). Note zoom scales around the CONTENT TOP-LEFT, not the viewport center — which is why zoom-to-cursor needs the compensating set_pan at worldsurface.py:229-237.

(2) EVERY GTK CONTROLLER / GESTURE (file:line, complete for the canvas area):
Canvas._setup_interactions (canvas.py:184-209):
- canvas.py:186-189 `Gtk.GestureClick` -> "pressed"=on_button_press (L463), "released"=on_click_released (L1140). No button filter, so it fires for all buttons.
- canvas.py:191-194 `Gtk.EventControllerMotion` -> "motion"=on_motion (L575), "leave"=on_motion_leave (L660). Drives hover state + cursor.
- canvas.py:196-200 `Gtk.GestureDrag` with `set_button(Gdk.BUTTON_PRIMARY)` -> "drag-update"=on_mouse_drag (L722), "drag-end"=on_drag_end (L1063). Move/resize/rotate/shear/rubber-band.
- canvas.py:202-205 `Gtk.EventControllerKey` -> "key-pressed"=on_key_pressed (L1313), "key-released"=on_key_released (L1337). `set_focusable(True)` + `grab_focus()` at L207-208.
WorldSurface.__init__ (worldsurface.py:70-94):
- worldsurface.py:70-74 `Gtk.EventControllerScroll.new(VERTICAL)` -> "scroll"=on_scroll (L201). ZOOM. Vertical only — no horizontal-scroll pan, no kinetic/smooth flags, no Ctrl-modifier branch.
- worldsurface.py:77-82 `Gtk.GestureDrag.new()` with `set_button(Gdk.BUTTON_MIDDLE)` -> "drag-begin"=on_pan_begin (L449), "drag-update"=on_pan_update (L455), "drag-end"=on_pan_end (L493). PAN.
- worldsurface.py:89-94 `Gtk.GestureClick.new()` with `set_button(Gdk.BUTTON_SECONDARY)` -> "pressed"=on_right_click_pressed (L112, overridden at surface.py:330). Context menu.
External, attached by MainWindow:
- rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/drag_drop_cmd.py:92-97 `Gtk.DropTarget.new(Gio.File, Gdk.DragAction.COPY)` added via `self.surface.add_controller(self._drop_target)`.
There are NO Gtk.GestureZoom (pinch), NO Gtk.GestureRotate, NO Gtk.EventControllerScroll KINETIC flags, and NO Gtk.ShortcutController anywhere in the canvas area. Touch/trackpad pinch zoom does not exist.

(3) SCREEN<->WORLD CONVERSION. One function: `Canvas._get_world_coords(widget_x, widget_y)` at canvas.py:171-182 — `self.view_transform.invert().transform_point((x, y))`, with a `np.linalg.LinAlgError` fallback that returns the raw pixel coords 1:1 (a silent wrong-coordinate failure mode worth noting). It inverts the matrix on EVERY call — no cached inverse. Its 20 call sites: canvas.py:480, 590, 596, 619, 738, 741, 818, 819, 1071, 1165, 1274, 1275; worldsurface.py:230, 232; surface.py:341, 592, 621; drag_drop_cmd.py:118; elements/tab_handle.py:143; and in addons (sketcher editor.py:180, sketchcanvas.py:452/521/549, print_and_cut pick_surface.py:181/239/242/281/309). World->screen has no single helper: callers compose `self.view_transform @ elem.get_world_transform()` inline (canvas.py:308, 333, 353, 635; element.py:373, 554; shrinkwrap.py:182; elements/workpiece.py:753) or call `view_transform.transform_point(p)` directly (axis.py:293/294/304/305/381-384/429/476; shrinkwrap.py:151; elements/outline.py:100+). A second, PARALLEL conversion API exists: `WorldSurface.get_view_scale()` (worldsurface.py:174-193) returns pixels-per-mm computed from get_content_layout * zoom_level WITHOUT going through the matrix — consumed at surface.py:266/816/822/842/914, elements/workpiece.py:670/829, elements/tab_handle.py:238, and in the sketcher addon (constraints/radius.py:209, hittest.py:245, renderer.py:61/141, tools/text_box_tool.py:895). These two paths can drift.

(4) VIEW-TRANSFORM CONSUMERS (file:line):
- Grid/rulers/axis labels: rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/axis.py:192 (param), 207 (invert for culling), 214 (get_scale -> adaptive grid size), 234/246 (dispatch), 293-305 (grid lines), 381-384 (axis lines), 429/476 (labels). Called from worldsurface.py:345-347 and surface.py:794-800.
- Selection frames: canvas.py:308 (`_draw_selection_frame`) -> overlays.render_selection_frame (overlays.py:312).
- Single-selection handles: canvas.py:333 (`_render_single_selection_overlay`) -> overlays.render_selection_handles (overlays.py:426); scale/flip compensation at overlays.py:459-461.
- Multi-selection handles: canvas.py:353 (`_render_multi_selection_overlay`); group hit-test scale at multiselect.py:170 and via canvas.view_transform.get_scale() in check_region_hit; min-resize-size in pixels at multiselect.py:245; flip flags at multiselect.py:257 and 300.
- Rotation handles / rotation math: canvas.py:627 and 630 (cursor angle during rotate uses `view_transform.get_rotation()`), canvas.py:635 (per-element screen transform for shear/resize cursor angles). Rotation snapping itself (canvas.py:884-905) is view-independent; it uses `SNAP_ANGLE_DEGREES = 5.0` (canvas.py:63) and world coords.
- Grid snapping: canvas.py:693 `_calculate_snap_offset`, invoked at canvas.py:834/837 (group move) and 863/866 (single element move), only while `self._ctrl_pressed`. Grid size = `self.grid_size`, set to 5 in Canvas (canvas.py:75) and overridden to 1.0 mm in WorldSurface (worldsurface.py:41). NOTE: the snap grid is a fixed 1 mm and is NOT tied to the AxisRenderer's adaptive visual grid — the visible grid and the snap grid disagree at most zoom levels.
- Resize/shear math: canvas.py:1002 and 1035 pass `view_transform=self.view_transform` into transform.resize_element / transform.shear_element, which use only `.is_flipped()` (transform.py:164, 176, 249).
- Element buffer sizing: element.py:373 (render resolution follows zoom).
- Element handle hit-test: element.py:554.
- ShrinkWrapGroup: shrinkwrap.py:151, 182.
- WorkPieceElement edit overlay: elements/workpiece.py:753.
- OutlineElement: elements/outline.py:100.
- Pipeline render context: surface.py:914 (`get_view_scale()` -> RenderContext.pixels_per_mm).

(5) ANIMATION / TICK / FRAME CLOCK. NONE in the 2D canvas, and effectively none in ui_gtk. Exhaustive grep for add_tick_callback / get_frame_clock / FrameClock / Adw.TimedAnimation / Adw.SpringAnimation / CallbackAnimationTarget / remove_tick_callback across rayforge/, tests/, scripts/ returns exactly ONE hit: rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-sketcher/sketcher/ui_gtk/studio.py:410 `self.add_tick_callback(grab_focus_callback)` — and that callback returns GLib.SOURCE_REMOVE immediately (studio.py:406-410), so it is a one-shot deferred focus grab, not animation. All time-based behaviour in the canvas is GLib timers: surface.py:254 (200 ms ops-restore debounce), element.py:348 (buffered-render debounce), drag_drop_cmd.py:294/592, elements/camera_image.py:164, elements/group.py:45, elements/workpiece.py:1127, shrinkwrap.py:33. A navigation rewrite that wants smooth/inertial zoom must introduce frame-clock driving from scratch — there is no precedent to follow in this package.

(6) TESTS. There are NO tests for canvas navigation (pan, zoom, reset_view, _rebuild_view_transform, on_scroll, on_pan_update), NO tests for snapping (_calculate_snap_offset, SNAP_ANGLE_DEGREES, grid_size), and NO tests for rotation (_start_rotation, rotate_element, rotate_from_drag). Confirmed by grepping tests/ for zoom|pan_x_mm|set_zoom|_calculate_snap_offset|SNAP_ANGLE|rotate_element|check_region_hit|view_transform — the only hits are tests/canvasapp.py (a manual demo app with zero test functions), tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_canvas_axis.py, tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_region.py, and tests/ui_gtk/sim3d/test_camera_controller.py (the 3D camera, a different subsystem). What DOES exist: tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_canvas_axis.py (16 tests of AxisRenderer layout/adaptive-grid/label drawing, all against Matrix.identity()); tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_region.py (8 tests of the MOVE gizmo rect + hit test, flipped and non-flipped); tests/ui_gtk/canvas2d/test_surface.py (1 parametrized WCS-marker test, GTK init bypassed); tests/ui_gtk/canvas2d/test_laser_dot_position.py (4 tests, real WorkSurface); tests/ui_gtk/canvas2d/test_projection.py (4 dataclass tests). Sketcher-addon tests mock `canvas.view_transform` (rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-sketcher/tests/ui_gtk/tools/test_{arc,circle,path,rectangle,select}_tool.py) — they will break if the attribute name or `get_scale()`/`transform_point()` contract changes.

WHAT BREAKS IF THIS SUBSYSTEM IS DELETED / SAFE REMOVAL ORDER.
Deleting the canvas subsystem breaks the app outright: MainWindow cannot construct (mainwindow.py:41 imports CanvasElement, mainwindow.py:42 imports DragDropCmd, mainwindow.py:281 builds WorkSurface, and ~40 further `self.surface.*` call sites through mainwindow.py:2045). It also breaks: the sketcher addon (SketchCanvas subclasses WorldSurface, sketchcanvas.py:34, plus hittest/renderer/tools that read view_transform and get_view_scale), the print-and-cut addon (PickSurface subclasses Canvas, pick_surface.py:25), the camera alignment dialog (CameraAlignmentSurface subclasses WorldSurface, alignment_widget.py:27), the array dialog (array_dialog.py:31-32, 63), doceditor/layer_cmd.py:187, rayforge/core/registration.py:138 (the context-menu extension registry lookup is a STRING path "rayforge.ui_gtk.canvas2d.context_menu" — it will fail at runtime, not import time), and the PROTECTED ruida simulator app (tests/machine/driver/ruida/simulator_app.py:20-21).

SAFE ORDER FOR A NAVIGATION REWRITE (not deletion):
1. Leave `Canvas.view_transform` (canvas.py:76) as the public contract. Five external consumers read it by name — canvas2d elements, shrinkwrap, multiselect, the sketcher addon (hittest.py:56/146/166, select_tool.py:465, snap_mixin.py:163/533), and 5 sketcher test files that mock it. Renaming or removing it is the single highest-blast-radius change available.
2. Leave `_get_world_coords` (canvas.py:171) and `get_view_scale` (worldsurface.py:174) signatures intact — 20 and 14 external call sites respectively, several in addons and one in a protected-adjacent tool.
3. Introduce a camera object BEHIND `_rebuild_view_transform` (worldsurface.py:257) first, keeping `zoom_level`/`pan_x_mm`/`pan_y_mm` as properties. Direct field reads exist outside the class: alignment_widget.py:75/76/79/91/167/170/183/191/319/324/351-355 and pick_surface.py:41-43/115/119/124/223/234/240/243-246/251-259, both of which reimplement the pan/zoom->pixel math by hand instead of using the matrix. Those two files will silently drift if the composition changes; fix or migrate them in the same pass.
4. Then unify the pan handlers. `WorldSurface.on_pan_update` (L455-491) and `WorldSurface.on_mouse_drag`'s space-branch (L411-438) contain byte-identical pan math; the mouse_drag branch uses `self.height_mm` for base_scale_y (L428) while `_rebuild_view_transform` uses `_axis_renderer.get_effective_height()` (L272-274) — these diverge in ROTARY mode, so space+drag panning is already subtly wrong on rotary machines.
5. Then touch the controllers. Adding gestures is safe; changing the existing four in `Canvas._setup_interactions` (canvas.py:184-209) affects every subclass, three of which (SketchCanvas, WorkSurface, CameraAlignmentSurface) override on_motion / on_button_press / on_key_pressed and call `super()`.
6. Keep `WorkSurface._suppress_ops`/`_defer_restore_ops` (surface.py:241-256) wired into whatever new pan/zoom entry points you create (currently surface.py:862-880). Without it, a frame-driven camera would call `_update_pipeline_view_context` (surface.py:899) — and therefore `view_manager.update_render_context` — every frame.

DOC/CODE MISMATCHES FOUND (docs are stale, do not treat them as spec): website/docs/reference/shortcuts.md:159 documents "Ctrl+Scroll = Precision Zoom" — no Ctrl branch exists in on_scroll. website/docs/ui/canvas-tools.md:52-54 documents Ctrl+"+", Ctrl+"-" and Ctrl+0 zoom shortcuts — no such key handlers exist anywhere (grep for KEY_plus/KEY_minus/KEY_0/KEY_equal/KEY_KP_Add returns nothing in rayforge/). The real reset-view key is '1' (worldsurface.py:367). canvas-tools.md:45-48 (middle-drag and space+drag pan) and :55 (wheel zoom at cursor) ARE accurate.

### External dependencies

- raygeo (pinned `raygeo==1.38.3` in requirements.txt:1) — provides `Matrix` (raygeo.geo), the whole view-transform abstraction. Used across the repo far beyond canvas: rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/drag_drop_cmd.py:16, rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/{group,stock,tab_handle,workpiece}.py, plus core geometry and pipeline. Matrix methods the canvas relies on: invert (18 uses), is_flipped (6), get_x_axis_angle (6), get_rotation (6), get_abs_scale (6), get_y_axis_angle (5), get_translation (4), get_scale (4), for_cairo (4), without_translation (2), has_zero_scale (2), plus Matrix.identity/translation/scale/rotation/shear and the @ operator. NOT canvas-exclusive — do not change its usage contract unilaterally.
- PyGObject / gi.repository (Gtk4, Gdk, Graphene, GLib) — used app-wide, not canvas-exclusive. Canvas-specific bits: Gtk.DrawingArea, Gtk.GestureClick, Gtk.GestureDrag, Gtk.EventControllerMotion, Gtk.EventControllerKey, Gtk.EventControllerScroll, Gtk.Snapshot, Graphene.Rect, Gtk.DropTarget.
- pycairo (`import cairo`) — canvas/{axis,canvas,cursor,element,hittest,overlays,shrinkwrap}.py and most canvas2d/elements. Also used elsewhere in the repo (rayforge/ui_gtk/camera/alignment_widget.py, sim3d).
- numpy — used in canvas/{axis,canvas,element,hittest,shrinkwrap}.py, mostly for `np.linalg.LinAlgError` when inverting a degenerate view matrix (canvas.py:178, axis.py:207) and for pixel buffers. Used repo-wide (pipeline, image, camera).
- blinker (`Signal`) — canvas/canvas.py:14 and canvas2d/surface.py:6 for the ~20 canvas signals. Used repo-wide as the app's event bus.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (6)

- tests/machine/driver/ruida/simulator_app.py:20 — `from rayforge.ui_gtk.canvas.worldsurface import WorldSurface`. The Ruida simulator UI (a PROTECTED path) builds a WorldSurface at tests/machine/driver/ruida/simulator_app.py:141-142 (`_create_surface`). Any change to the WorldSurface constructor signature, to `set_pan`/`set_zoom`/`reset_view`, or to the pan/zoom controller set will affect this protected tool.
- tests/machine/driver/ruida/simulator_app.py:21 — `from rayforge.ui_gtk.canvas2d.elements.dot import DotElement`. The Ruida simulator uses the canvas laser-dot element to track simulated position.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:899-947 (`_update_pipeline_view_context`) — reads `get_view_scale()` and pushes `RenderContext(pixels_per_mm=...)` into `self.editor.view_manager.update_render_context`. This is the canvas->pipeline VIEW render path (rayforge/pipeline/view/view_manager.py:314). It is adjacent to, but distinct from, the protected JOB path (intent_builder / build_rd_bytes / send_job / .rd export) — nothing in canvas/ or canvas2d/ references those. Changing when this fires (e.g. a smooth-zoom camera firing it every frame) would hammer the pipeline re-render.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:241-272 (`_suppress_ops` / `_defer_restore_ops` / `_restore_ops`, 200 ms GLib timer) — the throttle that keeps pan/zoom from re-rendering pipeline ops. A navigation rewrite that changes the pan/zoom entry points (surface.py:862-880) must keep this suppression wired or the pipeline render path will be re-triggered per frame.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:1368-1372 (`_center_on_rotary_axis`) calls `set_pan` and depends on `machine.panel` / layer rotary state — touches the layer/step + machine-panel system.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py:1201-1261 (`reset_view`) reads `machine.axis_extents`, `machine.work_margins` and MachinePanel axis directions — coupled to the machine/panel model that the jog panel and axis semantics also depend on. It does not write to them.

<details><summary>Module notes (33)</summary>

- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/canvas.py` - The base Canvas has NO zoom/pan concept — it only consumes a matrix someone else sets. tests/canvasapp.py is a manual GTK demo app (0 pytest test_ functions), not a collected test.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/worldsurface.py` - PROTECTED TOUCHPOINT: tests/machine/driver/ruida/simulator_app.py:20 imports WorldSurface. `set_show_grid` (L102) and `set_show_axis` (L107) have NO runtime caller anywhere — the win.show_grid action only reaches Canvas3D.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/surface.py` - Only ONE production instantiation: rayforge/ui_gtk/mainwindow.py:281.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/axis.py` - Not exported from canvas/__init__.py. WorldSurface._rebuild_view_transform, get_view_scale, on_scroll, on_pan_update and on_mouse_drag ALL call get_content_layout independently — 5 duplicate call sites of the same layout math.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/element.py` - Zoom changes invalidate every buffered element's surface — this is why WorkSurface suppresses ops during pan/zoom.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/region.py` - Takes `scale_compensation` (signed, flip-aware) so handles stay constant-size in pixels as zoom changes. This is the only canvas module with real unit tests.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/overlays.py` - Deliberately 'dumb' about the camera — it only receives a precomputed local->screen matrix. Good seam for a navigation rewrite.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/multiselect.py` - One of only two places outside canvas.py/worldsurface.py in the core canvas that reaches into canvas.view_transform for scale rather than receiving a matrix.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/transform.py` - Only consumes the view transform for its FLIP flag, never for scale/pan. Distinct from rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/property_providers/transform.py (unrelated file with a similar name).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/cursor.py` - Cursor angle is derived from the view transform in canvas.py:627/630/635 — the cursor rotates with view flip/rotation.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/hittest.py` - Not view-transform aware; operates in element-local/buffer space. Distinct from the sketcher addon's own hittest.py.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/intersect.py` - Called from Canvas._update_framing_selection (canvas.py:1266) after the frame rect is converted to world coords.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/shrinkwrap.py` - No production instantiation found — only the manual demo app tests/canvasapp.py:488 constructs one.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas/__init__.py` - AxisRenderer, transform.py, region.py, overlays.py are NOT exported — importers reach the submodules directly.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/context_menu.py` - Reached from WorkSurface.on_right_click_pressed (surface.py:330), which is bound by WorldSurface's secondary-button GestureClick (worldsurface.py:89-94).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/drag_drop_cmd.py` - Fifth controller on the WorkSurface widget, attached externally by MainWindow rather than in _setup_interactions.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/projection.py` - Currently inert w.r.t. navigation: the property exists on WorkSurface (surface.py:221/225) but no code reads `projection` to alter the view transform. A plausible hook for a future axis-remapping camera.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/ops_cache_registry.py` - Indirectly navigation-relevant: zoom changes re-render ops surfaces at a new pixels-per-mm and churn this cache.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/__init__.py` - No explicit importers; only implicit via `from .elements.X import Y`.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/workpiece.py` - The heaviest consumer of view scale — 1298 lines. Uses GLib.idle_add at L1127.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/tab_handle.py` - One of only two element modules that call _get_world_coords directly.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/outline.py` - Not used by WorkSurface itself; only by the Array dialog.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/crosshair.py` - Imports ElementRegion from ...canvas.region:18.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/dot.py` - PROTECTED TOUCHPOINT: imported by the ruida driver's simulator app.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/camera_image.py` - Synced by WorkSurface._sync_camera_elements (surface.py:1409), which is re-run on every reset_view.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/group.py` - Class defined at line 1-158 range; exact class line not separately confirmed beyond the grep of `class .*(CanvasElement)` which did not list it, so it subclasses something else in this file's chain.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/step.py` - Not imported by surface.py directly; reached only through layer.py.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/canvas2d/elements/nogo_zone.py` - Synced by WorkSurface._sync_nogo_zone_elements (surface.py:1374), re-run on reset_view.
- `tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_canvas_axis.py` - Never exercises zoom_level/pan_x_mm or _rebuild_view_transform — it passes Matrix.identity() as the view transform (L221).
- `tests/ui_gtk/canvas/test_region.py` - The only real canvas-interaction test file. It tests handle geometry, not navigation.
- `tests/ui_gtk/canvas2d/test_surface.py` - No view transform, no zoom, no pan.
- `tests/ui_gtk/canvas2d/test_laser_dot_position.py` - Closest thing to an integration test of the surface, but never touches navigation.
- `tests/canvasapp.py` - Zero `def test_` functions — not collected by pytest. Useful as a reference for a matrix-first navigation rewrite.

</details>

---

<a id="config-profile"></a>

## config-profile — machine profile persistence and config paths

### Summary

Config paths are computed once at import time in `rayforge/config.py`: `_get_config_dir()` honours `$RAYFORGE_CONFIG_DIR`, else `platformdirs.user_config_dir("rayforge")`, which on this Windows box resolves to `C:\Users\Robotics\AppData\Local\rayforge\rayforge`. Machine profiles live one-per-file as `<machine.id>.yaml` under `CONFIG_DIR/machines`, written by `MachineManager.save_machine()` via `yaml.safe_dump(Machine.to_dict(include_frozen_dialect=False))` and read back by `MachineManager.load_machine()` / `Machine.from_dict()`; the filename stem is authoritative for the id (`to_dict` never emits `id`). Separately there is a fully-working bundled-profile mechanism: `DeviceProfileManager` scans `rayforge/resources/devices/` (28 dirs, read-only, shipped) and `CONFIG_DIR/devices` (user, writable install target), each dir holding a `device.yaml` manifest (`api_version: 1` + `device:` meta + `machine:` MachineConfig) and an optional `dialect.yaml` (required only when the driver `uses_gcode`). Three Ruida profiles already ship — monport-60w-co2, omtech-polar, thunder-laser-nova35 — none with a dialect.yaml, which is correct since `RuidaDriver.uses_gcode = False`. On first launch with an empty config dir nothing is prompted: `rayforge/config.py` mkdirs every directory at import, then `RayforgeContext.machine_mgr` finds zero machines and silently calls `MachineManager.create_default_machine()`, which constructs a bare `Machine` (200x200 mm, GRBL dialect, one diode `LaserHead`) and saves it; `RayforgeContext.config_mgr` then picks `min(machines, key=id)` as active.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

This subsystem is load-bearing, not removable. Deleting rayforge/config.py alone breaks the app at import: 15+ modules pull path constants from it (context.py in 9 separate properties, logging_setup.py:10, debug.py:37, usage.py:12, addon_manager.py:29, app.py:64, a builtin addon at tool_library/__init__.py:17) and all three test-isolation fixtures in tests/conftest.py monkeypatch its attributes. Deleting rayforge/machine/models/manager.py removes the only writer for MACHINE_DIR and strands context.py:227 and :383, taking every machine-dependent test with it. rayforge/core/config.py owns config.yaml and is referenced by the pipeline (view_compute.py:12 imports OpsColorMode) and general preferences. The DEVICE-PROFILE half (rayforge/machine/device/**, rayforge/resources/devices/**) is the only genuinely severable piece: its dependents are exactly context.py:329-341, five UI files (settings/machine_settings_page.py, machine/unified_wizard.py + its wizard_pages, machine/profile_importer.py, machine/settings_dialog.py:334), and four test files. Removing it would cost the wizard's whole first page, the .rfdevice.zip import/export, the LightBurn importer, and all 28 bundled profiles. SAFE REMOVAL ORDER, if it ever came to that: (1) UI leaves — wizard_pages/profile_page.py, lbdev_import_dialog.py, profile_importer.py, the export handler at ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py:318-346; (2) unified_wizard.py and its call sites in ui_gtk/settings/machine_settings_page.py:195-223; (3) the context.device_profile_mgr property (context.py:329-341) and the two path constants config.py:54-55; (4) rayforge/machine/device/** and rayforge/resources/devices/**; (5) tests/machine/device/**, tests/machine/models/test_machine_profile.py, test_profile_driver_assignment.py, and the DeviceProfileManager uses in tests/core/test_model_manager.py:429/454/475. NEVER remove Machine.to_dict/from_dict or MachineManager — they are the on-disk contract for every existing user profile, including the live Ruida machine at C:\\Users\\Robotics\\AppData\\Local\\rayforge\\rayforge\\machines\\20fb2d0b-9637-4761-9331-286479d6307a.yaml. FOR THE EMBEDDING TASK SPECIFICALLY, the real risks are ordering and format, not deletion: (a) context.machine_mgr creates the anonymous 'Default Machine' the instant MACHINE_DIR is empty (context.py:228-229), and config_mgr triggers machine_mgr before it reads config.yaml — so any seeding must happen inside or before that empty branch, or the user ends up with two machines; (b) a bundled default expressed as a device.yaml can only set the 23 MachineConfig fields (profile.py:209-232) and cannot preset start_corner, active_wcs, axes, auto_connect, arc_tolerance, panel_orientation, reverse_*_axis, soft_limits_enabled, macros or coordinate_systems — a bundled default expressed as a pre-baked machines/*.yaml can set all of them but must then own the id/filename and will bypass all of the from_dict legacy migrations' intent; (c) laser head power percentages are 0-100 in YAML and 0-1 in memory (laser.py:187-188 vs :236); (d) fix `main_port` -> `port` in the three Ruida device.yaml files if the new default reuses them as a template, or it will inherit the same dead key.

### External dependencies

- platformdirs==4.11.1 (requirements.txt:9, pixi.toml:49) — imported at rayforge/config.py:5 (`user_config_dir`, `user_log_dir`) and independently at scripts/media/update_supporters.py:11. Those are the ONLY two importers in the repo.
- PyYAML==6.0.3 (requirements.txt:19) — used far beyond this subsystem. Within config-profile: rayforge/core/config.py:8, rayforge/machine/models/manager.py:6, rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:9, rayforge/machine/device/manager.py:9, rayforge/machine/models/dialect_manager.py:7, rayforge/app.py:62. Also used by the addon manager, recipe/material managers, and many tests — NOT removable with this subsystem.
- blinker==1.9.0 (requirements.txt:4) — Signal, used by rayforge/core/config.py:9, rayforge/machine/models/manager.py:7, rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:14, head.py:7. Used app-wide (doc model, canvas, drivers) — NOT exclusive to this subsystem.
- numpy==2.5.2 — head.py:6 and rotary_module.py use it for the 4x4 transform that gets flattened into the persisted YAML. App-wide dependency.
- raygeo==1.38.3 — machine.py:15-16 imports Point3D/Rect/Axis, which shape the persisted work_margins/soft_limits/axes values. App-wide dependency.
- PyGObject / gi (PyGObject==3.56.3) — only the UI entry points (ui_gtk/**) need it; the persistence core (config.py, core/config.py, machine/models/manager.py, machine/device/**) is GTK-free and importable headless.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (11)

- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:1678-1687 — `if ma.driver_name == "RuidaDriver" and ma.active_wcs == "MACHINE"` rewrites active_wcs to "REF0" on load, with a log line at L1683-1685. This is a one-way migration living inside the generic profile loader and it reads the protected driver by class name.
- rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:375-389 — `DeviceProfile.from_path` calls `get_driver_cls(machine_config.driver).uses_gcode` to decide whether dialect.yaml is mandatory. RuidaDriver.uses_gcode = False (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:61), which is the ONLY reason the three bundled Ruida dirs are allowed to omit dialect.yaml.
- rayforge/machine/device/profile.py:405-431 — `create_machine` uses the same uses_gcode check; for Ruida it takes the `elif not driver_uses_gcode: m.dialect_uid = None` branch at L430-431 instead of creating a custom dialect.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:210-236 — `RuidaDriver.get_setup_vars()` defines the driver_args contract a bundled Ruida profile must match: `host` (no default), `port` (default 50200), `jog_port` (default 50207). Read-only reference; do not edit.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:258-262 — `_setup_implementation` reads kwargs `host`/`port`/`jog_port`, confirming `main_port` in the bundled YAMLs is dead.
- rayforge/resources/devices/monport-60w-co2/device.yaml:17, rayforge/resources/devices/omtech-polar/device.yaml:17, rayforge/resources/devices/thunder-laser-nova35/device.yaml:17 — the `main_port: 50200` key. These are bundled resources, not driver code, but they are the profile-side half of the Ruida contract.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:183 / :1493 / :1731-1738 — `start_corner` default (TOP_LEFT), its serialization, and its parsing. Start-corner is protected jog/job-placement behavior; persistence merely round-trips it, and the live profile on this box currently holds `start_corner: bottom_left`.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:1508-1533 — persisted `speeds.max_cut_speed` / `max_travel_speed` (mm/min in the file; the jog panel and Min/Max power UI convert for display via rayforge/shared/units). Any bundled default must write mm/min, not mm/s.
- rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:1516-1518 — persisted `heads[].max_power` plus laser.py:186-201 `pwm_frequency`/`pulse_width` fields feed the protected Min/Max power and PWM UI.
- rayforge/machine/models/controller.py:202 — `new_driver.setup(**self.machine.driver_args)` is the hand-off from persisted profile data into the protected Ruida driver instance.
- rayforge/machine/models/controller.py:311-313 — `_on_driver_config_changed` writes driver-owned config back into `machine.driver_config`, which then persists into the machine .yaml.

<details><summary>Module notes (32)</summary>

- `rayforge/config.py` - VERIFIED on this machine: `python -c "from platformdirs import user_config_dir; print(user_config_dir('rayforge'))"` -> C:/Users/Robotics/AppData/Local/rayforge/rayforge. Log dir -> .../rayforge/rayforge/Logs. The mkdir calls run at MODULE IMPORT, so the directories always exist before any manager runs — 'empty config dir' never means 'missing dir'. Tests monkeypatch these module attributes (config.MACHINE_DIR etc.) and that works ONLY because rayforge/context.py imports them lazily inside each property body. Any bundled-default code must respect that same late-binding or it will break the test fixtures.
- `rayforge/core/config.py` - ConfigManager.save (L376-381) is plain `yaml.safe_dump` with no atomic rename. load (L383-419) tolerates missing/empty/corrupt file by falling back to a default Config.
- `rayforge/context.py` - Order matters: `config_mgr` touches `self.machine_mgr` first, so the default machine is always created before the config is read. A bundled default profile has to be seeded before or inside `machine_mgr`'s empty branch.
- `rayforge/machine/models/manager.py` - Because save_machine is wired to `machine.changed`, a machine created from a device profile is written to disk many times during `create_machine` (once per setter). `save_machine` writes non-atomically (open/write, no temp+rename).
- `rayforge/machine/models/machine.py` - PERSISTED FIELDS, all nested under a single top-level `machine:` mapping: name, driver (=driver_name), driver_args, driver_config, auto_connect, clear_alarm_on_connect, home_on_start, single_axis_homing_enabled, dialect_uid, active_wcs, coordinate_systems[] (CoordinateSystem.to_dict: name, optional label, offset[3]), supports_arcs, supports_curves, arc_tolerance, axes{configs:[{letter, axis_type, extents[2], direction, resolution, optional gcode_letter, optional rotary_diameter}]}, axis_extents[2], work_margins[4], soft_limits[4]|null, origin, start_corner, panel_orientation, reverse_x_axis, reverse_y_axis, reverse_z_axis, rotary_enabled_default, default_rotary_module_uid, wcs_origin_is_workarea_origin, heads[], cameras[], rotary_modules[], nogo_zones[], capabilities[]|null, hookmacros{TRIGGER: macro}, macros{uid: macro}, speeds{max_cut_speed, max_travel_speed, acceleration}, gcode{gcode_precision}, units{unit_system}, machine_hours{total_hours, counters{}}, plus optional frozen_dialect (suppressed when saving to disk). TWO ROUND-TRIP ASYMMETRIES CONFIRMED: (1) `id` is read in from_dict (L1636) but NEVER written by to_dict — the filename is the id. (2) `soft_limits_enabled` is read at L1768-1769 but is absent from to_dict entirely, so it silently resets to True on every load. DEFAULTS from __init__: name=_("Default Machine") L107; driver_name=None, driver_args={}, driver_config={} L131-133; auto_connect=True L136; home_on_start=False L137; clear_alarm_on_connect=False L138; single_axis_homing_enabled=True L139; dialect_uid="grbl" L140; gcode_precision=3 L143; supports_arcs=True L144; supports_curves=False L145; arc_tolerance=0.03 L146; unit_system=METRIC L147; max_travel_speed=3000 mm/min L153; max_cut_speed=1000 mm/min L154; acceleration=3000 mm/s^2 L155; axes X(0,200) Y(0,200) Z(-50,50) L156-174; work_margins=(0,0,0,0) L175-180; soft_limits=None L181; origin=BOTTOM_LEFT L182; start_corner=TOP_LEFT L183; rotary_enabled_default=False L185; soft_limits_enabled=True L187; wcs_origin_is_workarea_origin=False L188; active_wcs="G54" L195; coordinate_systems=CoordinateSystem.defaults() L196-198; `self.add_head(LaserHead())` L211.
- `rayforge/machine/models/head.py` - `_HEAD_SERIALIZED_KEYS` L14-16 is the known-key set; anything unknown is preserved in `extra` and re-emitted, so hand-written profile keys survive round-trips.
- `rayforge/machine/models/laser.py` - SCALE TRAP for a bundled profile: to_dict L187-188 writes `frame_power_percent = self.frame_power_percent * 100` and from_dict L236/L244 divides by 100. So in YAML these are 0..100, in memory 0..1. The existing bundled Ruida files use `frame_power_percent: 0.0` (= 0%) while the diode profiles use `1.0` (= 1%, not 100%). DEFAULTS (__init__ L30-48): max_power=1000, frame_power_percent=0, focus_power_percent=0, frame_speed=0, frame_repeat_count=20, frame_corner_pause=0, spot_size_mm=(0.1,0.1), cut_color='#ff00ff', raster_color='#000000', focal_distance=0.0, laser_type=DIODE, pwm_frequency=500, max_pwm_frequency=5000, pulse_width=50, min_pulse_width=5, max_pulse_width=500. `LaserType.supports_pwm` (L14-16) is True only for CO2 and FIBER — a Ruida CO2 profile therefore must set `laser_type: co2` to get PWM params.
- `rayforge/machine/models/spindle.py` - Only relevant as proof that the `type:` discriminator in a bundled head list works.
- `rayforge/machine/models/axis.py` - A device.yaml has NO `axes` key — only `axis_extents`. `Machine.from_dict` L1699-1719 falls back to `AxisSet.from_legacy(axis_extents, reverse_x/y/z, rotary_modules)` when `axes` is missing, which is the path a device-profile-created machine takes on its FIRST save (after which `axes` is written out).
- `rayforge/machine/models/coordinate_system.py` - Not settable from a device.yaml — MachineConfig has no coordinate_systems field.
- `rayforge/machine/models/machine_hours.py` - Runtime state, not profile data — a bundled default should leave it out.
- `rayforge/machine/models/rotary_module.py` - Settable from a device.yaml via `machine.rotary_modules:` (a list).
- `rayforge/machine/models/zone.py` - Settable from a device.yaml via `machine.nogo_zones:`.
- `rayforge/machine/models/macro.py` - IMPORTANT SHAPE MISMATCH between the two formats: in a machine .yaml `hookmacros` is a MAPPING {TRIGGER: macro}; in a device.yaml it is a LIST of macro dicts each carrying a `trigger` key (profile.py L262-271 writes it, L462-468 reads it).
- `rayforge/machine/models/dialect_manager.py` - Relevant because `DeviceProfile.create_machine` L420-429 turns a profile's dialect.yaml into a NEW custom dialect saved here, then points machine.dialect_uid at it. A Ruida profile skips this entirely (L430-431 sets dialect_uid = None).
- `rayforge/machine/device/profile.py` - MachineConfig fields (all Optional, omitted when None): driver, driver_args, driver_config, gcode_precision, supports_arcs, supports_curves, axis_extents(2), work_margins(4), soft_limits(4), origin, max_travel_speed, max_cut_speed, home_on_start, acceleration, single_axis_homing_enabled, rotary_enabled_default, unit_system, heads[], capabilities[], hookmacros[], rotary_modules[], nogo_zones[], cameras[]. NOT settable from a device.yaml (so a bundled profile cannot preset them): name-other-than-meta.name, auto_connect, clear_alarm_on_connect, arc_tolerance, dialect_uid (derived), active_wcs, coordinate_systems, axes, start_corner, panel_orientation, reverse_x/y/z_axis, default_rotary_module_uid, wcs_origin_is_workarea_origin, soft_limits_enabled, macros, machine_hours. `create_machine` calls `context.machine_mgr.add_machine(m)` at L403 BEFORE applying any config, so the machine hits disk with defaults first and is then re-saved on every setter. dialect.yaml is REQUIRED only when `driver_cls.uses_gcode` (L375-389) — RuidaDriver has uses_gcode=False, which is why the three Ruida dirs have no dialect.yaml.
- `rayforge/machine/device/manager.py` - Profiles are keyed by `pkg.name` (the human display name), NOT by directory name. Two bundled dirs with the same `device.name` would silently collide.
- `rayforge/machine/device/lightburn_importer.py` - A LightBurn-imported Ruida profile carries NO host/port driver_args at all.
- `rayforge/resources/devices/` - Full dir list: acmer-p3, acmer-s1, atomstack-a70, atomstack-x40-pro, carvera, carvera-air, creality-falcon-10w, creality-falcon-2-pro, creality-falcon-a1, elidor-z6, grbl-mks-dlc32, longer-ray5, monport-60w-co2, neje-master-3-max, omtech-k40plus, omtech-polar, ortur-laser-master-3, ortur-laser-master-4, sculpfun-c1, sculpfun-icube, sculpfun-icube-ultra, sculpfun-s30, sculpfun-s30-pro-max, sculpfun-s40-max, sculpfun-s70-max, thunder-laser-nova35, twotrees-tts55, xtool-d1-pro. Keys actually used across all bundled device.yaml under `machine:`: acceleration, axis_extents, capabilities, driver, driver_args, driver_config, gcode_precision, heads, home_on_start, max_cut_speed, max_travel_speed, origin, rotary_enabled_default, rotary_modules, single_axis_homing_enabled, supports_arcs, supports_curves, work_margins. Under `device:`: name, description, vendor, model.
- `rayforge/resources/devices/monport-60w-co2/device.yaml` - BUG CONFIRMED, worth fixing when embedding a bundled default: the key `main_port` exists ONLY in these three YAML files and NOWHERE in Python (`grep -rn main_port` over the whole repo returns exactly monport-60w-co2, omtech-polar, thunder-laser-nova35 device.yaml). `RuidaDriver.get_setup_vars()` (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:210-236) declares keys `host`, `port` (default 50200), `jog_port` (default 50207), and `_setup_implementation` L258-262 reads `kwargs.get("port", 50200)` / `kwargs.get("jog_port", 50207)`. So `main_port` is silently dropped and `jog_port: 50210` disagrees with the driver default 50207. The live working machine profile on this box uses the correct keys: `driver_args: {host: 192.168.1.100, jog_port: 50207, port: 50200}`. The three bundled Ruida profiles also omit `heads[].type`, which is fine — head.py:146-149 defaults a typeless head to LaserHead.
- `rayforge/resources/devices/sculpfun-s30/dialect.yaml` - Validated by `GcodeDialect.validate_template_dict` (profile.py L385-387). NOT needed for a Ruida bundled profile.
- `rayforge/machine/models/controller.py` - This is the path by which a bundled profile's driver_args actually reach the driver. `Driver.setup` (rayforge/machine/driver/driver.py:357-373) forwards **kwargs straight into `_setup_implementation`, which uses `kwargs.get(...)` — unknown keys like `main_port` are silently ignored rather than raising.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/settings/machine_settings_page.py` - This is the ONLY place a new machine is created interactively.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/profile_page.py` - This is where a new bundled default profile becomes visible to the user — no registration step needed beyond dropping a directory into rayforge/resources/devices/.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/wizard_pages/connection_page.py` - Because the required-key test is `var.default in (None, "")`, RuidaDriver's `host` (no default) is the only field the user must fill; port/jog_port come from the VarSet defaults 50200/50207 — NOT from the profile's `main_port`.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/settings_dialog.py` - Every edit here fires machine.changed -> MachineManager.on_machine_changed -> save_machine, so this dialog is the main write path into MACHINE_DIR.
- `rayforge/app.py` - `grep -rn "first_run|first-run|welcome|onboard"` over rayforge/ and tests/ finds only a GRBL serial 'welcome message' string — confirming no first-launch UI exists.
- `tests/machine/models/test_machine_profile.py` - ANY new bundled profile directory is automatically covered by this test. It is the cheapest guard on a new default profile's shape.
- `tests/conftest.py` - USER_DEVICES_DIR and BUILTIN_DEVICES_DIR are NOT patched by any fixture, so tests see the real bundled devices directory.
- `MANIFEST.in` - A new bundled profile directory needs no packaging change.
- `Rayforge.spec` - Confirmed no device-specific entries in debian/ or snap/ (`grep -rn 'resources|devices' debian/ snap/` returns nothing).
- `scripts/media/update_supporters.py` - The one place outside rayforge/config.py that duplicates the path computation. It does NOT honour $RAYFORGE_CONFIG_DIR.

</details>

---

<a id="branding"></a>

## branding

### Summary

Branding in this repo is already half-migrated: `rayforge/const.py:3` defines `APP_NAME = "Swift Cut"`, and seven call sites already route the window title, headerbar title, About dialog title/hero, file-dialog filter labels, the sketcher `.rfs` label, the debug-bundle banner and the update-available toast through it. What remains are (a) 16 user-visible strings that still hardcode the literal "Rayforge" (update-check messages, privacy/consent copy, addon-compat warnings, the About supporters blurb, a Patreon OAuth HTML page, an API-key request name), (b) the packaging/identity layer, which is uniformly `org.rayforge.rayforge` across the desktop entry, AppStream metainfo, MIME package, GTK `application_id`, macOS bundle id and snap `common-id`, and (c) the icon assets, which are still named `org.rayforge.rayforge.svg` / `rayforge.icns` / `rayforge.icon`. There is NO canvas watermark and NO log banner carrying the name — `docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:257` states this explicitly and I confirmed it (zero matches for "watermark" in `rayforge/`). Translations are gettext with one domain `rayforge`; the `.pot` and all seven `.po` files carry 11 msgids containing "Rayforge" and the `.po` headers carry "The Rayforge Project" copyright lines. No new SwiftCut icon asset exists anywhere in the repo — `docs/design/` contains only PNG screenshots and two markdown token docs, and `docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:140` (§2.2) records the deliberate decision to import no new assets.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

Branding is not a deletable subsystem — it is a name threaded through four independent layers, each with a different blast radius. Ordered from safest to most destructive:

TIER 1 — free (already done, or a one-line edit). `rayforge/const.py:3` APP_NAME is the seam the design doc designed for. Its 7 consumers (mainwindow.py:139, project_cmd.py:290/292, about.py:202/269/459, file_dialogs.py:240/246/273/284, debug.py:68, updater.py:65, sketcher exporter.py:21 / importer.py:43) all read it through `const.` and need no edit. Deleting APP_NAME breaks exactly those 7 modules at import time.

TIER 2 — safe but must be done in one pass with translations. The 16 remaining hardcoded user-visible strings (updater.py:44/82, general_preferences_page.py:279/345/353/363/408, usage_consent_dialog.py:13/16/25, about.py:418, addon_list.py:83, addon_dialog.py:215, patreon_provider.py:55, appkey.py:174, license_settings_page.py:182). Each edit CHANGES THE MSGID, which silently drops the existing translation in all 7 locales until scripts/update_translations.sh re-extracts and someone re-translates. This has already happened once and is live in the repo right now: updater.py:65 was changed to `_("{app} {version} is available.")` but rayforge/locale/rayforge.pot:31 still holds the old `msgid "Rayforge {version} is available."` — that toast is currently untranslated everywhere. Two of these strings cannot be translated at all: patreon_provider.py:55 is raw HTML bytes with no _(), and appkey.py:174 is a network payload, not UI. Correct order: edit strings → run scripts/update_translations.sh → fix the .po msgstrs (de/es/fr/pt/uk/zh_CN) → msgfmt.

TIER 3 — breaks existing installs. `rayforge/config.py:12/15/58` (RAYFORGE_CONFIG_DIR, user_config_dir("rayforge"), user_log_dir("rayforge")). Renaming these ORPHANS every user's machines/, dialects/, addons/, materials/, recipes/, color_presets/ and config.yaml with no migration path in the code. There is no migration helper anywhere in the repo. If this is ever renamed it needs a copy-forward step written first.

TIER 4 — breaks the ecosystem, and must move as one atomic set. The app id `org.rayforge.rayforge` appears in 8 functional places that MUST agree or the desktop integration silently degrades: rayforge/app.py:199 (GTK application_id), the data/org.rayforge.rayforge.desktop FILENAME, metainfo.xml:3 `<id>` + :37 `<launchable>`, Rayforge.spec:78 bundle_identifier, snap/snapcraft.yaml:10 common-id + :41, pyproject.toml:72-75 install paths, and about.py:263 which loads the icon BY THAT NAME. Specific failure modes: if the .desktop filename stops matching app.py:199, the shell shows a generic window with no icon and no app-grid association; if org.rayforge.rayforge.svg is renamed without updating about.py:263, rayforge/ui_gtk/icons.py:74-77 falls through to `Gtk.Image.new_from_icon_name()` and the About hero renders a broken/missing icon with only a DEBUG log line — no error. On top of that, `rayforge` is a claimed name in three external registries (PyPI via pyproject.toml:2, the Snap Store via snapcraft.yaml:1, Flathub via README.md:8) and a Debian source/binary package name (debian/control:1/:22, debian/changelog:1, debian/lintian-overrides:6); renaming means re-registering, not editing.

TIER 5 — must NOT be renamed. The gettext domain `rayforge` is hardcoded in app.py:84-85, in the 8 .po/.pot FILENAMES, in metainfo.xml:23, and throughout scripts/update_translations.sh (L143, L181, L198). Renaming it means renaming 8 tracked files and regenerating every .mo for zero user-visible benefit. Likewise the Python package path `rayforge.*` — 19,221 lowercase occurrences across the source tree plus 1,457 in tests, all python-module-path, plus the dynamic addon namespace `rayforge_addons.*` (addon_manager.py:749-830) and the manifest filename `rayforge-addon.yaml` (addon.py:16, addon_manager.py:428/1090/1240, update_translations.sh:208) which THIRD-PARTY ADDONS in the barebaric/rayforge-registry (config.py:68-69) ship on disk. Renaming the package or the manifest filename breaks every externally distributed addon.

MUST BE PRESERVED regardless of any rename: LICENSE (note it carries no copyright line at all), debian/copyright:7/:11 (Copyright 2023-2024 Samuel Abels), about.py:275 ("© 2025 Samuel Abels"), about.py:319-321 (Lead Developer row), about.py:323 (License MIT X11), the "Copyright (C) 2025 The Rayforge Project" + translator credit headers at lines 1-4 of each .po, metainfo.xml:5 (developer name) and :20-21 (metadata_license/project_license MIT), pyproject.toml:4-6/:9, debian/control:4.

ICON GAP: there is no SwiftCut icon asset in this repo. I checked docs/design/ (only PNG screenshots under audit/ and screens/, plus two .md token docs — no .dc.html, no SVG), rayforge/resources/icons/ (206 files, all -symbolic.svg utility glyphs plus the three branded app-icon assets), and website/static/images/. docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:140-153 (§2.2) records this as deliberate: the reskin imports no new assets. The three existing branded assets are org.rayforge.rayforge.svg (32KB, the live app icon), rayforge.icon/ (macOS 26 bundle, live, referenced from mac_build.sh:231-236), and rayforge.icns (465KB, tracked but a verified orphan — the build regenerates a root-level copy from website/static/images/icon-app.svg via scripts/mac/mac_create_icon.sh:22-24). Note also that BOTH platform icons derive from website assets, not from rayforge/resources: Windows from website/static/images/favicon.png (win_create_icon.py:6), macOS legacy from website/static/images/icon-app.svg. A new SwiftCut icon therefore has to land in at least three places — resources/icons/, the .icon bundle's Assets/, and the website static images — or the Linux, Windows and macOS builds will disagree about what the app looks like.

### External dependencies

- PyGObject / gi (Adw, Gtk, GLib, Gdk, Gio) — used by about.py, mainwindow.py, usage_consent_dialog.py, icons.py. Used by essentially the whole ui_gtk tree, not just branding.
- aiohttp — rayforge/updater.py:7, for the GitHub Releases API call behind the 'Rayforge is up to date' / '{app} {version} is available' messages. Also used elsewhere in the repo (listed in debian/control:29 as a runtime dep and used by the octoprint driver).
- blinker (Signal) — rayforge/updater.py:8 notification_requested, and rayforge/logging_setup.py:8. Used broadly across the repo.
- platformdirs (user_config_dir, user_log_dir) — rayforge/config.py:5, which is what makes the config dir literally named 'rayforge'. Only config.py imports it.
- PyYAML — rayforge/debug.py (yaml.safe_dump for the debug bundle). Used across the repo for machines/materials/addon manifests.
- Pillow (PIL.Image) — scripts/win/win_create_icon.py:3, only for generating rayforge.ico. Build-time only; not a runtime dep of the app.
- gettext (stdlib) + the gettext toolchain (xgettext/msgmerge/msgfmt/msguniq/msgattrib, >= 0.25 per scripts/update_translations.sh:21) — the whole translation layer. Build/dev only.
- PyInstaller — Rayforge.spec and hooks/hook-rayforge.py. Build only.
- NSIS — scripts/win/win_installer.nsi. Build only.
- Docusaurus (website/) — website/docusaurus.config.js carries its own independent 'Rayforge' branding (title L20, blog title L53, JSON-LD L134/L175/L193, OG/Twitter meta L201-219, footer copyright L268 'Rayforge Contributors'). It is a separate deployable, not imported by the app.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (11)

- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:616 — `path = Path(tempfile.gettempdir()) / "rayforge_last_job.rd"` inside `_dump_job_blob()` (L612-626). This is a BRANDED FILENAME living inside the protected Ruida driver, on the send_job path. The user sees it in the log line at L623-625 ('Job blob written to {path}'). An untracked artifact of this exists at the repo root: rayforge_last_job.rd. Report only — do not touch the driver.
- data/org.rayforge.rayforge.xml:12-15 — registers `application/x-ruida` with `<glob pattern="*.rd"/>` and comment 'Ruida laser cutter file'. Renaming this FILE (it is named after the app id) changes where pyproject.toml:74 and snap/snapcraft.yaml:71 install the Ruida MIME association. The .rd export/open association depends on it.
- data/org.rayforge.rayforge.desktop:9 — `MimeType=...;application/x-ruida;...` — the .rd file association for the launcher.
- scripts/win/win_installer.nsi:76-79 — registers HKCR `.rd` → `Rayforge.RuidaFile` with description 'Ruida Laser Cutter File'; uninstall counterpart at :141. Renaming the ProgID prefix rewrites the Windows .rd association.
- rayforge/const.py:4-5 — MIME_TYPE_PROJECT / MIME_TYPE_SKETCH ('application/x-rayforge-project', 'application/x-rayforge-sketch'). Consumed at rayforge/ui_gtk/project_cmd.py:248 (recent-files entry) and by the sketcher addon at exporter.py:23 / importer.py:45. These sit next to the .rd export path in the same const module — editing const.py for APP_NAME means editing a file the protected export path also reads.
- rayforge/builtin_addons/rayforge-addon-sketcher/sketcher/image/exporter.py:21 and importer.py:43 — `label = _("{app_name} Sketch").format(app_name=const.APP_NAME)`. Already parameterised, so an APP_NAME change silently retitles the .rfs importer/exporter entries in the import/export UI. Adjacent to, not inside, the protected .rd job path — but worth naming because the label change is invisible in a diff of these files.
- docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:257 and :275-276 — the protected design doc explicitly scopes branding: 'there is no watermark to rename', and 'Branding changes APP_NAME in rayforge/const.py only. Module and package names stay rayforge; there is no code rename.' Any branding work that touches package names contradicts a protected document.
- docs/design/swift-cut-tokens.md:272 — commit plan row C lists 'branding → "Swift Cut"' as part of the same commit as the Cut Scale sheet, job-progress and Stop-red work, verified by 'protected-behaviour tests + handler-count test' (tests/ui_gtk/test_swift_cut_handlers.py). Branding changes are covered by that test's contract.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/theme.py and rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py — the Swift Cut token modules. I verified they contain NO app-name string: theme.py's 'Rayforge' hits are only the docstring at :2/:12/:301/:347/:391/:402 and layout.py's at :7/:12. Branding and the theme tokens are cleanly decoupled; const.py imports nothing from either.
- rayforge/resources/icons/file-rd-symbolic.svg — the Ruida file icon. Unbranded name, no change needed; listed so it is not swept up in an icon rename.
- tests/ui_gtk/test_mainwindow.py:105 and tests/ui_gtk/test_project_import.py:78 — both build `f"org.rayforge.rayforge.test.{test_name}"` as the test Gtk app id. Any change to the application_id at rayforge/app.py:199 must be mirrored here or the UI tests drift from the app.
