<a id="pipeline-protected"></a>

## pipeline-protected — the Ruida job path (Doc → ops → RuidaEncoder → .rd blob → send_job / export), plus the jog panel and the unit/axis conversion sites

### Summary

The protected path is a single linear chain with exactly one fork at the end. `IntentController._rebuild` (rayforge/pipeline/intent_controller.py:390) runs `IntentBuilder.build` (rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:169) on a worker thread; the builder emits five kinds of raygeo `NodeRequest` — one `workpiece:{wp}:{step}` compute node per workpiece/step pair, one `step:{uid}` aggregate, one `job` aggregate, one `job:machinexform` compute node, and one `job:encode` node — wired by stable key with SHA-1 version tokens as the sole cache-invalidation signal. `Pipeline._on_job_encoded` (rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py:350) turns the finished `job` aggregate ops plus the encode node's text/op-map into a `JobArtifact` in the `ArtifactStore`. From that one artifact the path forks: `MachineCmd._start_job` → `RuidaDriver.run` (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:540) calls `build_rd_bytes(ops, machine, doc)` (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_encoder.py:101) and hands the blob to `RuidaClient.send_job` (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_client.py:260), while `FileCmd.export_rd_to_path` (rayforge/doceditor/file_cmd.py:1009) calls `export_rd` (ruida_encoder.py:112), which is `build_rd_bytes` plus a `write_bytes` — so the exported file is byte-identical to the transmission by construction, and tests assert it (tests/machine/driver/ruida/test_ruida_encoder.py:1306, tests/doceditor/test_file_cmd.py:1194, tests/machine/driver/ruida/test_ruida_driver.py:1480). Note the driver deliberately ignores `encoded.driver_data` and re-encodes from `ops` (ruida_driver.py:545-553), because the pipeline reconstructs `EncodedOutput` from raygeo's `EncodeOutput.MachineCode`, which carries only text and op-map. The jog panel (rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py) is a separate, non-job motion surface that shares only `MachineCmd` and the `Machine`/driver objects; the only place it touches the job path is Start (`run_send_job`) and the two Scale buttons, which pull the same `JobArtifact` for its bounding box.

### Coupling risk / safe removal order

WHAT BREAKS IF THIS IS DELETED — the whole app. This is not a leaf subsystem; it is the spine. Concretely, in order of blast radius:

(1) rayforge/pipeline/encoder/base.py is the single highest-fanin file in the protected area: EVERY driver in the repo (dummy, grbl×4, marlin, octoprint, smoothie, ruida) plus machine/cmd.py plus intent_builder.py import EncodedOutput/OpsEncoder from it. Touching its signatures breaks all seven driver families at once, not just Ruida.

(2) rayforge/pipeline/transformer/base.py + registry.py are the plugin surface. All 9 transformers in rayforge-addon-post subclass OpsTransformer, all 6 laser steps and core/step.py:23 import transformer_registry, and rayforge/core/registration.py:39 names "rayforge.pipeline.transformer.registry" AS A STRING — a rename fails silently at addon-registration time rather than at import.

(3) rayforge/pipeline/stage/assembler_helpers.py is imported by 6 files in rayforge-addon-laser. Its own __init__.py says it is the last survivor of the old stage package; it looks vestigial and is not.

(4) The .rd export and the send share exactly one encode function. export_rd (ruida_encoder.py:112) is literally build_rd_bytes + write_bytes, and three tests assert byte-identity (test_ruida_encoder.py:1306, test_ruida_driver.py:1480, test_file_cmd.py:1194). Any change that makes the two paths diverge is a silent correctness failure that only shows on hardware.

(5) rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_util.py is shared with the .rd IMPORTER (rayforge/image/ruida/parser.py:3). Changing swizzle/encode primitives to fix export breaks import.

(6) The Ruida driver is registered ONLY by the import at rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:20 plus the `isdriver` locals() scan at L24-32. Dropping that import de-registers the driver with no error.

VERIFIED-SAFE-LOOKING BUT ISN'T: RuidaDriver.run deliberately ignores `encoded.driver_data` and re-encodes from ops (ruida_driver.py:545-553), because the pipeline reconstructs EncodedOutput from raygeo's EncodeOutput.MachineCode which carries only text + op_map. Anyone "optimising away" the double encode will ship an empty blob.

GENUINELY LOW-COUPLING, IN REMOVAL ORDER (safest first) — these are the only parts I would even discuss:
  a. rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_simulator.py — verified ZERO inbound references from rayforge/; only tests/machine/driver/ruida/{test_ruida_simulator.py, simulator_app.py} use it. Removing it costs the dev simulator and two tests, nothing in the app.
  b. rayforge/pipeline/view/** (view_manager.py, view_compute.py, __init__.py) — inside the pipeline package but NOT on the job path; only doceditor/editor.py:19 consumes it, for the 2D canvas preview. Removing it kills workpiece previews, not the job.
  c. rayforge/pipeline/artifact/step_ops.py — the only rayforge/ inbound is the package __init__ re-export at artifact/__init__.py:4; nothing else imports StepOpsArtifact. It IS part of the public package API, so removal is an API break even though nothing consumes it today.
  d. RuidaClient.set_speed / set_axis_speed (ruida_client.py:~640/~649 via _build_speed L792 / _build_axis_speed L811) — verified NO caller in ruida_driver.py; only tests exercise them.

ORDERING RULE if anything is removed: leaves first (simulator → view → unused client speed setters), never the encoder contract, never the transformer registry, never the driver __init__ import. And nothing in rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/**, intent_builder.py, build_rd_bytes, send_job, the .rd export chain, or jog_widget.py should be touched at all under the current protection rules.

TWO CORRECTIONS TO THE MOTION_AUDIT CLAIMS THE BRIEF ASKED ME TO VERIFY:

mm/s — the claim is essentially TRUE but stated too broadly. mm/min is the app base unit (rayforge/shared/units/definitions.py:89-96, set_base_unit at L111) and mm/s is display-only (L102). Conversion to display happens once, in UnitSpinRow.get_value_in_base_units / set_value_in_base_units (rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/pref_rows/unit_spin_row.py:131/136), and the jog panel deliberately does NOT round-trip through it (bottom_panel.py:511-515). On the wire there are exactly TWO mm/min → um/s encoders, one per path, and each is genuinely the only one for its path: RuidaDriver._set_travel_speed (ruida_driver.py:1090, the arithmetic at L1099) for interactive motion, and RuidaEncoder._speed_to_um_s (ruida_encoder.py:240) for the job — the latter is the only caller of the C9 02/C9 03/C9 04 speed encodings in the encoder (L452, L1056, L1195, L1200). BUT: there is a THIRD mm/min → um/s arithmetic site at rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:1498 (`speed_um_s = max(1.0, speed_mm_min * 1000.0 / 60.0)` inside `_wait_for_jog_settled`). It is a local timeout estimate, not a wire encoding, so it does not falsify the audit's byte-level claim — but the audit's blanket wording "the only place mm/min becomes um/s" is wrong as written. Separately, RuidaClient._build_speed (ruida_client.py:792) and _build_axis_speed (L811) take mm/s, not mm/min — a second unit convention living inside the client. They are currently unreachable from the driver, so nothing is broken today; they are a trap for the next editor.

Axis mapping — the claim of a SINGLE site is FALSE as a global statement; it is TRUE per path, and there are three paths. (i) JOB path: rayforge/machine/models/coordspace.py:217 `MachineSpace.get_world_to_machine_matrix` composes reverse_x/reverse_y (entering at L213-214) into the 4x4 that IntentBuilder puts on MachineTransformSpec.world_to_machine (intent_builder.py:857-890), so the Rust machine-transform stage flips signs exactly once for the whole job. (ii) JOG path: rayforge/machine/models/machine_panel.py:418 `MachinePanel.calculate_jog` — its docstring correctly claims "the one place a visual direction becomes a delta", and it rotates through the same world→machine matrix (L429); Z is delegated to Machine.calculate_jog (rayforge/machine/models/machine.py:900). (iii) CONTROLLER path: RuidaDriver._to_controller (ruida_driver.py:1338) and _axis_um_from_controller (L1345) apply reverse_x_axis/reverse_y_axis again when talking to the controller and when reading positions back. Plus _axis_end (L1329-1333) reads them for travel limits. So five sign-flip sites in the motion path total, three of them in the Ruida driver. They do not conflict — each operates in a different coordinate space — but "there is a SINGLE site" is not what the code says, and anyone consolidating them on the strength of that sentence will introduce a double flip.

### External dependencies

- raygeo (raygeo.ops, raygeo.geo, raygeo.pipeline.request/stage/execute/completed, raygeo.cnc.execution.specs/intent, raygeo.ops.convert, raygeo.image.grayscale, raygeo.ops.part/state/types/axis) — the Rust core. Imported by intent_builder.py:50-70, intent_controller.py:40-46, pipeline.py:13-14, ruida_encoder.py:13-16, machine/cmd.py, jog_widget.py:4. NOT exclusive to this subsystem: 264 files under rayforge/ import raygeo. Removing anything here cannot free this dependency.
- numpy — intent_builder.py:49 (rotary matrix math), artifact/base.py:6, artifact/workpiece_view.py:6, encoder/base.py:5, stage/assembler_helpers.py:19, view/view_manager.py:9, view/view_compute.py:6, machine/models/coordspace.py, machine/models/machine_panel.py. Also used across the whole repo (core, canvas, simulator).
- blinker (Signal) — pipeline.py:12, intent_controller.py:39, transformer/base.py:6, view/view_manager.py:10, ruida_client.py:13, ruida_transport.py:10, ruida_simulator.py:23. 118 files under rayforge/ import blinker; not exclusive.
- PyGObject / gi (Gtk, Gdk, GLib, Graphene, Gsk, Adw) — jog_widget.py:3, cut_scale_dialog.py, bottom_panel.py, file_dialogs.py, main_menu.py, actions.py, mainwindow.py. Whole UI layer; not exclusive.
- stdlib only for the Ruida byte layer: ruida_maps.py has ZERO imports; ruida_util.py, ruida_codec.py, ruida_protocol.py import only stdlib + sibling modules. ruida_encoder.py adds raygeo + pathlib. This is the most cleanly isolated part of the protected path.

### PROTECTED touchpoints (19)

- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:982 — `driver_encoder = driver_cls.create_encoder(machine)`; for a Ruida machine this returns `RuidaEncoder()` (rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:256-257). The pipeline reaches INTO the protected driver package here.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:72-73 — `from ..machine.driver import get_driver_cls` / `from ..machine.driver.dummy import NoDeviceDriver`. The pipeline imports the driver registry, which at rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:20 imports RuidaDriver.
- rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py:984-1000 — the `encode(ops)` closure that calls `driver_encoder.encode(ops, machine, doc)` and converts EncodedOutput → raygeo EncodeOutput.MachineCode. This is where the Ruida encoder actually runs, on a rayon worker thread under the GIL.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_encoder.py:18-22 — RuidaEncoder imports EncodedOutput / MachineCodeOpMap / OpsEncoder from rayforge/pipeline/encoder/base.py. The protected driver depends on the pipeline's encoder contract.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_encoder.py:23 — `from ..driver import acceleration_run_up_mm`, used in `_collect_job_info` bounds computation (encoder L825).
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:18 — `from ....pipeline.encoder.base import EncodedOutput, OpsEncoder`.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:553 — `blob = build_rd_bytes(ops, self._machine, doc)` inside `run`. THE send-path encode call.
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:591 — `await self._client.send_job(blob, on_start=..., on_chunk=...)`, wrapped in `_polling_suspended()` (L586) with a 0.2 s pre-sleep (L590).
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:576 — `if not await self._move_to_start_corner(ops)` — the start-corner pre-move gates the send; a job is REFUSED (not guessed) when the position cannot be established (L989-991).
- rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py:945 — `corner = self._machine.start_corner`, read from the Machine model the bottom panel's start-corner toggles write (bottom_panel.py:498).
- rayforge/doceditor/file_cmd.py:49 — `from ..machine.driver.ruida.ruida_encoder import export_rd`. The doceditor reaches directly into the protected driver package; this is the ONLY non-driver rayforge module that does.
- rayforge/doceditor/file_cmd.py:1038 — the `export_rd(artifact.ops, machine, self._editor.doc, file_path)` call.
- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:190 and :197 — `await machine.driver.run(encoded, self._editor.doc, ops, on_command_done=...)`, the two branches (granular vs non-granular progress). Everything that runs a job passes through one of these two lines.
- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:759 — `driver_cls.create_encoder(machine)` inside `_create_driver_encoder`, used ONLY by the Cut Scale path (`_scale_job` L579-581), which encodes synthetic ops outside the pipeline.
- rayforge/machine/cmd.py:302 — `_run_send_action` hands `artifact.ops` + `artifact.encoded_output` to `_execute_monitored_job`; artifact.ops is what build_rd_bytes re-encodes.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py:6 — `from ...machine.cmd import MachineCmd`; and L7 `from ...machine.models.machine import JogDirection, Machine, Origin`. The jog panel's entire dependency on the machine layer.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py:8-15 — imports JOG_CELL, SPACE_CONTROL, SPACE_GROUP, SPACE_TIGHT and `format_position` from rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py (PROTECTED Swift Cut layout tokens). jog_widget.py:47-48 derives the whole panel height from JOG_CELL.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py:398 — `self.machine.panel.calculate_jog(direction, self.jog_distance)`, the jog axis-mapping hop into machine_panel.py:418.
- rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/bottom_panel.py:26 — `from ..machine.jog_widget import DEFAULT_JOG_SPEED_BASE, JogWidget`; the panel seeds its Jog Speed row from the widget's own constant (L429) so panel and driver agree before any user input.

<details><summary>Module notes (58)</summary>

- `rayforge/pipeline/intent_builder.py` - Steps 1+2 of the brief. Key formats are centralised at L94-101 (WORKPIECE_KEY_FMT / STEP_KEY_FMT / JOB_KEY / JOB_ENCODE_KEY / JOB_MACHINEXFORM_KEY) with helper functions L103-136. The Ruida encoder is reached at L962 `_make_python_encoder_callable` → L982 `driver_cls.create_encoder(machine)`; Grbl short-circuits to a native Rust GcodeSpec at L823. `_approximate_job_ops` (L1155) exists only to populate dialect template extents for Grbl.
- `rayforge/pipeline/intent_controller.py` - `_audit_job_ops` (L90) is a job-ops sanity audit run inside this module.
- `rayforge/pipeline/pipeline.py` - `_drop_job_handle` (L421) is the safety valve: a generation that produces no job releases the previous artifact so a stale blob cannot be sent or exported.
- `rayforge/pipeline/artifact/job.py` - Docstring at L44-51 states the coordinate conventions; it still references a `Machine.encode_ops()` that the machine-transform node has since replaced.
- `rayforge/pipeline/artifact/step_ops.py` - Only inbound reference inside rayforge/ is the package __init__; tests/pipeline/artifact/test_step_ops_artifact.py also imports it. Candidate for closer review if anyone proposes removals, but it IS exported from the package API.
- `rayforge/pipeline/artifact/workpiece_view.py` - Canvas preview only. Lives in the pipeline package but the .rd blob never touches it.
- `rayforge/pipeline/__init__.py` - No rayforge/ module imports the package root by name — everything imports submodules directly. Only a test imports `rayforge.pipeline` itself. Deleting it would still break the package.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/base.py` - HIGHEST-FANIN module in the protected area. Every driver in the repo depends on it.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/context.py` - Gcode-side only; the Ruida path does not use it.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/gcode.py` - Not on the Ruida path, but shares encoder/base.py with it.
- `rayforge/pipeline/encoder/rust_helpers.py` - Imported unconditionally by intent_builder, used only on the Grbl branch (`_grbl_encoder_spec` L823).
- `rayforge/pipeline/transformer/base.py` - The whole rayforge-addon-post addon subclasses this. Removing it removes every post-processor.
- `rayforge/pipeline/transformer/registry.py` - rayforge/core/registration.py:39 names the module by string — a rename breaks addon registration silently.
- `rayforge/pipeline/transformer/placeholder.py` - Lazily imported inside a method to avoid a cycle.
- `rayforge/pipeline/stage/assembler_helpers.py` - rayforge/pipeline/stage/__init__.py is an empty comment file that explicitly says this is the last module of the old stage package.
- `rayforge/pipeline/stage/__init__.py` - Contains no code, only a docstring-style comment.
- `rayforge/pipeline/status_messages.py` - Only consumer inside rayforge/ is intent_controller. Couples to intent_builder via `parse_workpiece_key` (L19) and to the step registry.
- `rayforge/pipeline/assembly_warnings.py` - Imported lazily inside `DocEditor._on_assembly_warnings`. Reached via Pipeline.assembly_warnings (pipeline.py:94, emitted at pipeline.py:305).
- `rayforge/pipeline/view/view_manager.py` - NOT on the .rd job path. It lives inside rayforge/pipeline/ but consumes only workpiece artifacts; the job aggregate, machine transform and encode nodes never reach it.
- `rayforge/pipeline/view/view_compute.py` - Same as view_manager: canvas preview only.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_encoder.py` - Signatures: `build_rd_bytes(ops: Ops, machine: "Machine", doc: "Doc") -> bytes`; `export_rd(ops: Ops, machine: "Machine", doc: "Doc", path: Path | str) -> None`; `commands_to_rd_bytes(commands: list[bytes]) -> bytes`. Conversion sites: `_mm_to_um` L236, `_speed_to_um_s` L240 (mm/min → um/s), `_power_to_ruida` L251. Min/Max power: `_handle_set_power` L321, `_min_power_for` L355, `_layer_min_power` L366 (resolves the floor by STEP uid first, layer uid second, warns loudly at L419).
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_driver.py` - `create_encoder` (L256) returns a bare `RuidaEncoder()` — this is what IntentBuilder calls at intent_builder.py:982 and MachineCmd at cmd.py:759. Registered as a driver purely by the import at rayforge/machine/driver/__init__.py:20 plus the `isdriver` scan at __init__.py:24-32; there is no explicit registry entry.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_client.py` - `_build_travel_speed` (L788) takes um/s; `_build_speed` (L792) and `_build_axis_speed` (L811) take mm/s and multiply by 1000. That is a SECOND unit convention inside the client (mm/s, not the app base mm/min) — see coupling_risk.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_util.py` - CROSS-SUBSYSTEM: rayforge/image/ruida/parser.py (the .rd IMPORTER) imports this module. The importer and the exporter share swizzle/encode primitives.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_maps.py` - No third-party imports at all; pure data.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_transport.py` - Uses absolute imports (`from rayforge.machine.transport...`) unlike its siblings.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_server.py` - MOTION_AUDIT.md explicitly flags this as NOT ground truth.
- `rayforge/machine/driver/ruida/ruida_simulator.py` - No rayforge/ module imports it — it is test/dev-only. Verified: `grep -rn ruida_simulator rayforge/` returns nothing outside the ruida package itself.
- `rayforge/machine/cmd.py` - `_cut_scale_ops` (L725) synthesises a one-layer Ops for Cut Scale and `_create_driver_encoder` (L750) encodes it OUTSIDE the pipeline — the one place a job runs without going through IntentBuilder. `_warn_if_job_overruns_bed` L89 is the bed-overrun guard.
- `rayforge/doceditor/file_cmd.py` - `export_gcode_to_path` (L961) sits directly above it and shares `assemble_job_in_background`.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/jog_widget.py` - Hold-jog: `_attach_hold` L268 wires a CAPTURE-phase GestureClick (pressed L614 arms a 200 ms timer, released L622, cancel L634) plus a motion 'leave' controller; `_start_hold` L607 → `_press_jog_key` L540 → `machine_cmd.jog_key_down`. Safety releases: `_release_all_jog_keys` L566 called from `_on_jog_leave` L639, `_on_jog_gesture_update` L644 (drag-off inside the implicit grab), `_on_unmapped` L663, `_on_root_active_changed` L676 (window focus loss), `_on_connection_status_changed` L735, and `set_machine` L363 (machine swap). Speed push is debounced 300 ms: `set_jog_speed` L680 → `_commit_jog_speed` L695. `DEFAULT_JOG_SPEED_BASE = 12000` at L28 (= 200 mm/s in base mm/min).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/machine/cut_scale_dialog.py` - Only consumer is jog_widget. Seeded with `machine_cmd.first_layer_power() * 100.0` (jog_widget.py:873).
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/doceditor/bottom_panel.py` - Start corner writes straight to the Machine model (`machine.set_start_corner` L498), which the Ruida driver reads at ruida_driver.py:945.
- `rayforge/machine/models/machine_panel.py` - Note the arrow-sign inversion at L797-800 of the docstring block (presented_deltas: EAST = -distance). This is intentional and load-bearing for the diagonals.
- `rayforge/machine/models/coordspace.py` - IntentBuilder feeds the result into MachineTransformSpec.world_to_machine (intent_builder.py:890) so the Rust machine-transform stage does the flip once, for the whole job.
- `rayforge/shared/units/definitions.py` - This is where the mm/s convention is DEFINED. Everything downstream carries mm/min.
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/shared/pref_rows/unit_spin_row.py` - The jog panel deliberately never round-trips through the display unit (bottom_panel.py:511-515 comment): 'Converting to the display unit and back used to quantise every setting to whole mm/s.'
- `rayforge/ui_gtk/layout.py` - PROTECTED (Swift Cut theme + layout tokens). The jog panel's whole geometry (5 rows × JOG_CELL) derives from JOG_CELL at jog_widget.py:47-48.
